**And the projects column is a third narrower — 240 → 160.** It is a list of names you already know; the page beside it is where the work is, and eight controls in a row over there were competing for width with a column spending it on the tails of project names. A name that no longer fits is cut with an ellipsis, and every row already carried the whole name in its hover.

‼️ **It was not 240 either.** `min-width` was never set, and a flex item's automatic minimum size is its CONTENT's — so the basis was being ignored and the column stood at whatever its widest child wanted, measured at 283px while the rule said 240. Both halves were checked by measuring the rendered page rather than by reading the rule.

## 3.122.0 — One card per session, and an ended one can be continued from it (2026-08-13)

**S-29.** An ended session keeps its card in the Console's left panel until you press **Remove from list**, and it keeps it for two reasons: the conversation is still readable, and the session is under your hand when you want it back. Only one of those actually worked.

**Resuming it built a second card.** Whichever door you came in by — the card, the resumable list underneath, a session row on another page — the panel ended up holding two cards with the same session id, one live and one grey, and doing it again would have made three. The seat is now the host's own question: a card that has ENDED is the seat the resumed session takes (same hosted id, so a selection and a right-click menu keep pointing at the session they were pointing at), and a card that is still RUNNING is handed back untouched rather than spawning a second process on one transcript. It is decided in `ConsoleHost.start`, so all six routes get it — one of them had guarded half of it by hand and the other five guarded neither. Every engine, because nothing about it is Claude-shaped.

**And the card can now continue its own session** — `Continue This Session Here`, first in the card's right-click menu and greyed until the session has ended. It is the same call every other resume route makes: the earlier conversation comes back with it, so does what it has already spent, and so does its own project (the appended context does not survive a resume, so that spawn is the one that has to re-tell it). A session that ended before the engine ever named it says so instead of failing quietly.

`test_console_seat.js` drives the real `ConsoleHost` with a fake child rather than grepping the source for a word: end and resume three times over and there is still one card, a live card is handed back without a spawn, and two unrelated sessions are still two cards.

## 3.121.4 — The backlog's control row stops rocking under the hand (2026-08-13)

**A-14.** `search text or code · state: 2/4 ▾ · filing: 📥 unfiled ▾ · queue order (yours) · ↑ · cut at` was a row that moved every time you touched it. Three separate causes, and all three are fixed where they belong rather than on this one page.

**A picker now reserves the widest text it could ever print.** That width is knowable when the control is drawn — its label, its "all" word, `none`, `n/total` and every option — so `AFFilter.multi` measures it once and writes a fixed width. Choosing `filed — has a code` used to grow the button by forty characters, push the tail of the row onto a second line, and pull it back when the choice was undone.

**An option may be a sentence in the open menu and only its head on the button.** In the menu it has a line to itself and you are choosing between them; on the button it is a readout. The cut lands on the dash those sentences use, never mid-word, and the hover still carries the whole thing — it is the first line of the panel, as it has been since K-16.

**The inset bar and re-tinted fill are gone.** They arrived with the search rebuild and applied to every picker in the extension. The button already says a filter is set, in words, in the one place a reader is looking; a second signal for the same fact, on a small button with rounded corners, read as a stray bracket beside the box.

**And the ordering box is the same size as everything beside it.** It was the one control in the strip nobody had styled, so it inherited 13px next to 12px pickers and 11px badges — and it printed whole clauses (`family — parents, then their parts`), which is what decided how much of the row it took, permanently, at the search box's expense. Every ordering is now named in a word or two; the clause is on each option in the open list and is the first line of the box's hover for whichever ordering is chosen.

## 3.121.3 — One search engine, and every box says so (2026-08-13)

**X-37.** The Search page was rebuilt with a real grammar under it and a Syntax card beside it, and the question afterwards was the obvious one: *are the other boxes the same?* Two were not, and neither of them looked wrong.

**The Session Viewer's message filter and the Task Analytics table searches were still substring matches** — `hay.toLowerCase().includes(typed)` — on two of the longest lists in the extension. That box could not ask for two words that are not next to each other, could not exclude anything, and could not take a phrase; on a transcript of six hundred messages that is the whole point of having one. Both now compile through `media/afQuery.js`, once per pass rather than once per row, and both are given the RAW box value, because the grammar decides its own case handling. `prompts.list` on the bridge was matching with `includes()` as well, so the two doors onto one list answered differently; it now uses the same grammar as the picker's own filter.

**The hover text is built from the Syntax card's own rows now.** `SYNTAX` was a second hand-written list whose comment claimed to be derived from `SYNTAX_ROWS` and was not — so the card grew `"a* b*"` (a phrase whose words may each be partial) and no hover in the extension ever learned it, while every hover went on telling readers to turn on a **Whole word** toggle that exists on one page out of eleven. The flat text is now generated from the rows, in the card's own three columns, and it closes with what a bare word actually does in a form any box can act on: *put it in "quotes" when it has to stand on its own.*

**And it is swept rather than remembered.** `test_query_boxes.js` fails on any `media/` file that decides a match with a lowercased substring, and on any page that compiles a query without offering the grammar on its box — with one declared exception, the Search page, which draws the grammar in full one press below the box and therefore does not also whisper it.

## 3.121.2 — A hover never opens towards the edge it is already on (2026-08-13)

**K-18.** The `?` under the Console's composer sits a few pixels off the BOTTOM of the window, and its panel hung *downwards* out of it — so the thing it exists to explain was almost entirely off screen. Same mistake, other axis, as a 760px panel opening rightwards off a trigger already three quarters of the way across a row.

This is now a rule the component keeps rather than a thing each call site has to remember: `afTip.js` measures the anchor and the panel the moment the pointer arrives — before the open delay has elapsed, so nothing is ever seen moving — and writes `af-up` / `af-left` where the default side does not fit and the other one is roomier. Where neither fits, the roomier half still shows more. A page's own `tip-right` and `tip-block` are statements about a LAYOUT and are never overruled; a `tip-free` panel is placed in full by its own placer and is left alone.

Driven in a real Chrome to check it against something other than a string: a trigger 6px off the bottom of a 984×705 window now opens a 122px panel that ends at 684, and one against the right edge ends at 978. Held by `test_tip_repaint.js` (both edges, both opt-outs, and a re-measure after a repaint moves the row) and written into `docs/CONVENTIONS.md`.

## 3.121.1 — The saved-prompts picker stops covering its own list (2026-08-13)

**U-54.** Every row in the Console's saved-prompts window carried a rich hover holding the whole prompt. In a list you are *reading in order*, that is a panel opening over the two neighbouring prompts you are comparing it against — the reader already asked for this window, so nothing has to be teased out of it. The rows keep their label and 120-character preview; the whole text is one click away in the composer, and `✎ Edit the file` opens the source. The hover rule in `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` is about a row you merely pass over on a page, and that distinction is now written down where it belongs.

## 3.121.0 — X-35, properly: Gemini gets a transport, and the audit's 23 findings are closed (2026-08-13)

An independent read-only audit of the three X-35 sessions found that the item had been closed on the six surfaces the last session had listed, not on the product. It recorded 23 open defects — 4 critical, 12 high, 7 moderate — against a suite that was 8,308 green, and named the reason: most multi-engine tests proved that a word existed in the source rather than that a function behaved. Every finding was re-verified against `HEAD 220f6be` before being fixed, and each fix is now covered by a test that runs the code.

**Gemini was not half-integrated — it was wired to the wrong program.** It had an engine row, a model list and a place in every picker, and no transport: the dispatch read "if Antigravity … else Codex", so a Gemini turn was handed `codex exec --json --skip-git-repo-check` and whatever came back was parsed as Codex JSONL. There is now a measured `gemini.ts` (argv) and `geminiproto.ts` (the `init`/`message`/`tool_use`/`tool_result`/`error`/`result` stream), read from gemini-cli 0.55.1's own binary, bundled docs and source. Two measurements would have been copied wrong from the neighbouring engine and are asserted directly: its stats are **per process, not cumulative** (the opposite of Codex, so Codex's running-total subtraction would have zeroed every turn after the first), and its `input_tokens` **does** include the cached share (the opposite of Antigravity, so it has to be subtracted). Its own oddities are stated rather than smoothed over: no reasoning-effort flag at all, so the box is not drawn and passing one is refused; `ask_user` resolves to **deny** on an unattended turn; and a headless run that *leaves* plan mode switches to yolo. The dispatch is now exhaustive — a fifth engine without a transport is a compile error, not a silent fallthrough.

**A correct bridge contract had been silently overwritten by an older one.** `triggers.add` was rewritten to carry the four-engine run dimensions and registered a second time further down the same file, flat `model` only; `Map.set` let the second win. The source read correctly, the tests passed on the source, and the running system used the version with no engines. `templates.add` and `templates.list` were doubled the same way. The three are merged into one superset handler each, and **a duplicate registration now throws at startup** — one stack trace instead of a mystery months later.

**"How many sessions does this project have?" had two official answers.** The rollup counted the Claude-only pool while the project's own Sessions tab listed all four engines: measured on this machine, 260 against 300, with the last-activity date stuck on an older Claude session while the newest work on the project was a Codex one. Counting, ownership and recency are now the record's job across every engine; cost and budget stay on the pool that can be added up, and `measuredSessions` states the gap instead of hiding it. The same split fixes the live rows, which reported "not found" as `tokens: 0, turns: 0` on a running Codex session with 186 tool calls and 137,779 tokens in its own transcript.

**A transcript's filename is not its session id on three engines out of four.** Codex writes `rollout-…-<uuid>.jsonl` and Gemini `session-1.json`, both stating the real id inside — so every map keyed on a basename simply never matched, and automated foreign runs showed 🧑 User as their origin while the Session Viewer focused the wrong terminal. One resolver each way (`byId`, `byFile`) replaces a dozen copies of a Claude-only lookup, including the one that made a resumed Codex thread pay for its whole history on its first new turn.

**External terminals reach the Live Timeline.** Codex, Gemini and Antigravity fire no hooks, and only the old custom-CLI path ever opened a lane, so a first-class launch on any of them ran with nothing on the page. The launcher now opens a lane immediately under a synthetic id and **re-keys it in place** when the transcript states the real one — keeping the counters, the start time and the history — with the terminal binding and project stamp following it.

**Reusable work remembers how to run.** Templates and workflow steps now carry engine, model, effort, permissions, CLI, working folder, priority and project, on the page and over the bridge: a task set up on Codex and saved as a template used to come back as Claude every time. The Missions page gained per-mission and per-member engine pickers and a visible token ceiling — on a team delegating to a subscription engine the dollar limit can never be reached, so the only real fence had been invisible.

**Money says only what is known.** "This CLI prints no price" had been turned into "you are on a plan, so this was free" — a financial claim nothing had checked, since Codex and Gemini each sign in with either a subscription or an API key. Capability, figure-provenance and billing arrangement are now three separate things, and the Gemini row's confident, dated claim that Google sign-in "was retired in June 2026" is corrected against the shipped CLI, which still offers it.

**Farm Status stops calling a four-engine farm Claude-only.** Built-in engines and custom CLI profiles hung on one condition, so with no profiles configured the board announced "sessions and tasks are Claude-only" on a machine holding 39 Codex sessions. Two independent rows now, and the `cliProfileCount: 0 + externalSessions > 0` case — never tested before — is asserted. `Start Session With…` no longer offers a built-in engine as a custom profile, and the marketplace description, README and Tasks empty state name the four engines and the two honest limits (only Claude quotes a price; only Claude fires live hooks).

Three new test files run the code rather than reading it: `test_gemini.js` (argv and protocol against the measured vocabulary), `test_four_engine_acceptance.js` (duplicate-op ban, engine validation at write time, dimension round-trip, project count parity, the Farm Status case) and `test_session_identity.js` (both resolvers across four engines, and the record/measurement split still intact). 140 files, 8,424 assertions, TypeScript clean.

## 3.120.0 — X-35 closes: the last surfaces that could only see Claude (2026-08-12)

**The sidebar stops keeping two answers.** An agent's `Sessions` header counted the Claude-only list while the rows underneath it were Claude plus every foreign engine, so the number and the list it sat on disagreed — worst on exactly the agent that had moved to Codex. Both now come from one list, and the header states its own cut (`15 of 42`) instead of applying it silently; the hover names the engine split.

**A foreign session is no longer a second-class row.** The `session-external` bypass — no right-click menu at all, a click that dumped raw JSONL into an editor, the engine printed as its bare id — dated from before any of it worked another way. Every reason for it is gone: the viewer parses Codex, Gemini and Agent Farm's own Antigravity transcripts, Resume asks the engine before opening a terminal and says so when the answer is no, and rename/pin/tag reach the side list. Every row is built one way now; the differences that are real fall out of the row's own facts (no working folder → not resumable, no transcript → the click explains why, in that engine's words). The hover states cost in the terms the engine reports: tokens where no price is quoted, never `$0.00`.

**"Working right now" is a log fact and answers for every engine.** `isAgentActive` read the Claude-only list, so an agent that spent the afternoon on Codex was drawn idle — grey dot in the sidebar, `active: false` on its portfolio row, its dashboard card and the bridge — beside a last-activity date, read from the widened list, saying it had been busy minutes ago. Fixed at the source, so the four surfaces cannot drift apart again; the status bar's "N sessions across M agents" moves with it.

**The Turns page can open and export the rows it shows.** The table widened to every engine in 3.119.0 and every lookup behind it still went to the Claude-only list: a Codex turn you could see and tick refused to unfold and was dropped out of the Markdown export. The `turns.list` op takes an `engine` filter naming all four — over a Claude-only pool, asking it for Codex returned an empty array that read as "Codex did nothing".

**Findings say what they could not see, and say it once.** The scope sentence rode on the two fleet rows; the row somebody actually acts on is the agent's — "6 sessions this week and never read DUTIES.md" is a verdict on the whole week drawn from a quarter of it. It is now a field rather than a tail on the message, so the sidebar keeps the finding in the label and the sentence in the hover, and the agent page prints it once above its findings table instead of eight times down it. It appears only for an agent that actually has foreign sessions. The Cost Anomaly Report, headed "fleet / all sessions / heavy hitters", carries the same sentence in the same words.

**Four more surfaces that quietly meant "Claude".** `projects.sessions` promised "the data behind the page's Sessions button" and returned a strictly smaller set than that page shows; the session shelf listed pinned rows from every engine above a "Recent" half none of them could enter; the farm-status board's recent list omitted every Codex conversation, so a board read from another machine said the farm was idle; and a project track row written from a foreign session lost the turn number that makes it locatable later.

The agent page gained its first render fixture — the one major surface with no picture — and was reviewed in dark and light themes. Verified with 8,303 automated assertions.

## 3.119.0 — Multi-engine work is documented honestly (2026-08-12)

**The guide now describes the product that exists.** Claude Code, Codex, Gemini and Antigravity are first-class engines selected from the Engine box; `agentFarm.cliProfiles` is the deliberately smaller escape hatch for an arbitrary command Agent Farm does not understand. The old instructions that told readers to add Codex as a profile — and that Agent Farm could never host Antigravity — were left behind by the architecture and have been replaced in both English and Turkish.

**The coverage boundary is explicit.** Record surfaces use the complete multi-engine log; Claude-shaped analytics stay on the data they can genuinely measure and state what they exclude. The guide now includes the practical setup flow, a surface-by-surface coverage table, per-engine resume/transcript/plan-limit behaviour, Codex's cumulative token accounting, same-engine task attribution and the reason a plan-billed engine shows tokens instead of a fictional `$0.00`.

**X-35 remains open, with a precise handoff.** The updated remaining-work note records what sessions `38de7845-c46d-4f3b-bef7-aac0119a249b` and `a9b5b11a-a4bb-4a04-b9bc-e33217ac5e33` actually completed and isolates the six code gaps still requiring a later coding pass. This release changes documentation and delivery metadata only; no source, media or test code was changed.

## 3.118.0 — The Search panel stops moving (2026-08-12)

**The query box is one line, and the caret stays in it.** It was a two-line textarea that could be dragged over the buttons below it; it is now a fixed-height single-line field, which is what a query is. More importantly, the live search no longer evicts the cursor: the page rebuilds its whole card on every change, so the answer arriving used to throw the caret out of the box and the next letter went nowhere. The focused control is now noted before each repaint and given back after it — with its caret position — and a chip pressed from the keyboard comes back under the keyboard. `Enter` runs the search.

**The box carries no hover any more.** A paragraph that opened every time the pointer crossed the one control you are using was noise, especially with a `Syntax ▾` card one press below. Everything the hover knew and the card did not — what the box actually searches, what each mode does to the grammar, the ten-second regex budget, what Meaning does not cover, what Whole word is doing while it is on — was moved into the card.

**Three bands, and none of them collide.** The panel now reads how (mode, case, whole word, syntax) · where (opening scope, agents, session id, filters) · what (quick scope and the content badges). The content block is two fixed rows: the purple `Quick scope` pill, then the badges — six content kinds and the two extra sources as separate runs, so a narrow window moves them as groups rather than orphaning one badge. The old grid ran the badges underneath the Filters button at one width and stretched an empty purple box across half the row at the next; neither can happen now.

**Each clearer answers for the block it sits in, and nothing shifts when it appears.** `Reset` used to light up for filters it does not touch and sat at the opposite end of the row from the badges it puts back, with `Clear filters` at the end where the reset belonged. Now: a ✕ at the end of the badge row resets the content scope only, a ✕ beside the Filters chip clears the session axes only, and both live in slots that hold their width whether the button is there or not. Measured: the Filters chip and the badge row occupy identical geometry with nothing set and with two filters set.

**Opening a panel is no longer a change of question.** Opening the syntax card or the filter panel used to mark the answer on screen as stale, and changing the sort asked the host again for hits the page already held. `Aa Case` and `▣ Whole word` do the opposite of what they did — they re-run the search instead of telling you to press a button the page could have pressed. A live search now dims the previous answer rather than blanking the page for the length of every scan.

Two fixes to the looking glass came out of this: `render_page.js` now puts a page back inside `#app`, so a page that caps its own width there is photographed at the width the user actually gets (every earlier picture of Search was 200 px too wide), and `lib_page_dom.js` models focus — a repaint drops it exactly as a browser does, so the caret assertions cannot pass on a dead node.

Verified with 8,214 automated assertions and Chromium renders at 1600, 1000, 880 and 780 px in dark and light themes.

## 3.117.0 — Search controls stop fighting the search (2026-08-12)

**The query owns the row.** It is now a full-width, two-line textarea that may grow vertically, with only the fixed Search button beside it. Text and Regex searches are live again after a 500 ms pause; `Ctrl/⌘+Enter` runs immediately, while Meaning remains deliberate because it may spend a paid embedding call. Clearing or resetting invalidates an answer already in flight, so old results cannot return under an empty query.

**Compact badges stay compact and stay put.** The visible terms are the product's own short vocabulary — **You · Agent · Delegator · Handler · Tools · Results** — with the full distinction in rich, scrollable AFTips. **Conversation · Delegation · Tool trail · Everything** now occupy a purple-framed `Quick scope` group instead of blending into the role switches. Selected colours differ by function, while reserved check space, unchanged font weight and a fixed Filters width prevent any press from resizing or shoving the controls. At narrow widths roles move as a stable group instead of wrapping one badge at a time or clipping the last choices.

**The hidden panel no longer hides its way out.** Session id/title/path gets the wide flexible field. When any session filter is active, **Clear filters** remains outside beside the closed Filters badge; **Reset** returns the query, mode, roles, sources, scope and all filters to their initial state. Engine choices now come from the single product engine registry, so **Claude, Codex, Gemini and Antigravity** remain present even when one engine has no session in the current pool.

**Known session ID is a direct door.** `Find session ID…` is visible in Search and opens the existing whole-index finder, seeded from the query. Full IDs and eight-character prefixes work across every engine; the detail-panel Session field remains what it actually is — a way to narrow a text scan.

Verified with 8,198 automated assertions plus Chromium inspection at 1400, 900 and 700 px in dark and light themes.

## 3.116.0 — Search is compact again (2026-08-12)

**The search page is a search page, not a three-step wizard.** The numbered Ask / Choose content / Narrow sessions cards and the six oversized content tiles are gone. Query + Search, query mode, content scope and the agent picker now fit into one compact control panel; the first result begins immediately below it.

**The badges were not the problem.** Conversation, Delegation, Tool trail, Everything, all six message kinds, Memory and Events are compact badges again. Their behaviour remains repaired: plain click adds/removes, Ctrl/⌘-click keeps only one, and a selected badge holds its check mark, fill, focus border and `aria-pressed` state after the pointer leaves. The full explanation stays in the wide, scrollable `AFTip` rather than consuming page height.

**Detailed session filters fold away as one unit.** Agent remains in the main row. Engine, Project, Model, Session id/title/path, Started by, Lane, Pinned, Errors, date and sort open together under `⚙ Filters (n)` and collapse back to that one badge. The four coverage figures are one thin result strip instead of four cards. At the 1180 px visual fixture the closed control panel measures 115 px and the first result starts at y=219.

Verified with 8,184 automated assertions and inspected Chromium renders of the compact and expanded Search panel in dark and light themes.

## 3.115.0 — Search is now one coherent workspace (2026-08-12)

**Search was rebuilt as a three-part workspace.** The old query row and patchwork of tiny badges are gone. **Ask** separates Text, Regex and Meaning; **Choose content** gives Conversation, Delegation, Tool trail and Everything plus six full message-kind cards; **Narrow sessions** keeps Agent, Engine, Project, Model and Session visible, with the less common axes one level below. A selected control stays selected after the pointer leaves: fill, focus border, check mark and `aria-pressed` agree in dark and light themes.

**The explanations finally use the rich tooltip the rest of the product already proved.** The query box and every multi-picker use the shared wide, scrollable `AFTip`; native `title` no longer carries their paragraphs. The query explanation was rewritten in plain language and the complete grammar remains available as a proper table under **Query guide**.

**A preview cap can no longer pretend to be the answer.** Transcript search now counts all matching messages after the per-session preview limit, and returns **matches found · previews shown · sessions matched · sessions scanned · omitted matches · parse failures**. The default fleet preview is 12 per session / 250 total; every session card states `shown of found`, and timeout, unreadable transcript and omitted-preview coverage are explicit.

**The session scope now uses the session's real project.** Project filtering previously mapped agent name to one default project, which is false as soon as one agent works in more than one project. Search and `search.transcripts` now resolve each session through explicit membership, code root and agent default in the same order as the rest of Agent Farm.

**Meaning search now honours the filters it shows.** Allowed session IDs are applied to the vector pool before top-k ranking; filtering twenty global hits afterwards can no longer throw away the valid answer. The workspace also states the mode's actual boundary — indexed main-session conversation passages, not tools, memory, events, message dates or engines absent from the index — and disables unsupported content controls rather than silently ignoring them.

**Repeated exact searches no longer re-parse the fleet.** Claude and foreign transcripts share a bounded revision cache keyed by mtime, size, engine and lane. One user record with several text blocks remains one turn; normal text beginning with `<` is searchable; known harness envelopes alone are excluded. The bridge returns the same coverage counters as the page.

Verified with 8,183 automated assertions and inspected Chromium renders of the complete Search workspace in dark and light themes.

## 3.114.0 — Search is a search: six kinds, every engine, and the id you already have (2026-08-12)

**Search now reads all four engines.** The scan was written against Claude's JSONL and quietly stayed there, so a fleet that had been running on Codex, Gemini and Antigravity for weeks was searchable on one CLI out of four and nothing on the page said so. Foreign transcripts are now read with the very parser the Session Viewer reads them with (`core/cliViewer`), each result row carries its **engine badge**, and a repeated search re-parses nothing — a foreign file is parsed once per change, not once per keystroke. (X-24, X-35)

**‼️ A delegating agent is not a user.** In a subagent lane the prompts are written by the agent that handed the work over, and they arrived wearing the word `user`: asking for "only what I asked" answered with paragraphs one agent wrote to another, and no surface could tell them apart. There are two kinds now, read off the transcript's own `isSidechain` rather than guessed — **Delegator**, by the name of the agent that wrote it (`delegator (manager)`), and **Handler** for the side that answers. The Session Viewer labels them the same way (`📤 DELEGATOR (developer)` / `📥 HANDLER`), and so does Export MD, so the screen and the file never name a speaker differently. Dialog therefore means you and the agent; the delegation lane is one press away and the results line always says which kinds were searched and how many were not. (X-25)

**The Search page has filters at last.** Everything a session *is* sat in the index unusable while the page offered a date: **Engines · Projects · Models · Started by · Lane (main / sub-agents) · ★ Pinned · ⚠ Has errors · Session id · When · Sort**, behind a `⚙ Filters (n)` chip that counts what is narrowing. They narrow **the set that is scanned** rather than sieving hits afterwards, so "n sessions scanned" means what it says and the scan gets cheaper; the pickers are built from what the fleet actually holds, so a new engine, project or model appears with no code change. The same axes are on the bridge, through the page's own scope function rather than a second copy of the rules. (X-24)

**‼️ The kind chips are badges again, and a badge toggles.** They had briefly come to mean "only this one" on a plain click, which made the one thing the row exists for impossible — searching two kinds at once, which the engine has accepted since the day it was written. A click adds or removes; Ctrl/⌘-click means only that one; the scope can never empty; and the row no longer prints "→ the conversation" at its end, because which chips are lit *is* the answer. (U-42)

**`whole word`, and a grammar you can read.** Whole-word matching existed only inside quotes — a rule nobody can discover by looking at a search box. It is a mode now, one tick, applied to bare words exactly as quotes apply it, with `*` still opening whichever end it is written on; the box says so in its hover while it is on. The grammar itself moved out of a one-line tooltip into a **help card** behind `?` — symbol, meaning and an example each — **and its examples are in the language of the interface**, which the Turkish ones in an English extension were not. (X-24)

**‼️ "I know the id, take me there."** Every surface prints eight-character session ids and there was no way back from one. Paste an id (or its prefix) into the search box or the Session filter and a link to that session is drawn above the results, engine badge and all — without narrowing the search, because an id in a box is as often "where was this mentioned" as "open it". From anywhere else: **`Agent Farm: Find Session by ID…`**, which ranks the whole index exact id → prefix → mention across every engine, plus `sessions.find` on the bridge. The Sessions tables' text filter matches the id too. (X-34)

**The filter panel gets the hovers its table already had.** The rows below it carried rich, repaint-proof panels while the panel itself carried thin native tooltips — and three of its controls carried none at all. Worse, every filter change rewrites that block, and a native tooltip does not survive a repaint under a stationary pointer: the one you were reading vanished and did not come back. Every explanation there is now the shared rich panel, and the range boxes say what the column means *and* what is currently set. (X-33)

Verified with 8,172 automated assertions and real Chromium renders of the Search page (results · filter panel · help card) and the Sessions filter panel in dark and light themes.

## 3.113.0 — Ten small doors: the Console names what it is holding, and the backlog says which size it means (2026-08-12)

**"Resume an existing session" is every engine's list now, not Claude's.** The Console built it from the deliberately Claude-only half of the session index, so every conversation the user had had with Codex, Gemini or Antigravity was unreachable from the one panel whose job is to continue a conversation — and nothing on the panel said so. It reads both halves; each row wears its engine badge, Claude included; the text box narrows on the engine name (`codex` shows only those); and a resumed row starts on the engine it was recorded with, with the composer's model and effort narrowed to what that engine actually offers rather than passed through to be refused. (K-17)

**A notice from a project session files itself.** Agents post to the board through `notices.post`, and the `project` argument was one nobody remembered to pass, so the work the user had themselves asked for under a project landed as "No Project" and filing it fell back to them, one row at a time. When the caller says nothing, Agent Farm now stamps the project the posting agent is actually working in — a session this window hosts, a session the live monitor can see, or a project that declares that agent by rule — and the answer says it did (`projectFrom`). It only fills in when the answer is unambiguous: an agent in two sessions under two projects still posts unfiled, because picking one would be a decision about the user's filing that nobody made. `project:"(none)"` is how a poster says "this belongs nowhere" out loud. (N-1)

**A control's hover says what is IN it before it says what it is for.** The Console composer is eight pickers wide in a panel that is rarely wide, so a `<select>` showed its value cut and the only way to read it was to open the box — the gesture for "I am about to change this", performed by somebody who only wanted to look. Every picker in the extension now leads its hover with the current value (a multi-pick names its whole selection instead of counting it: `show: You · Agent`, not `show: 2/7`), then a blank line, then the standing explanation it always carried. (K-16)

**An effort no longer travels to a model that has never heard of it.** Reading the effort list per model was already done; what was missing is that changing one of the pair has to settle the other. On the Tasks form the Effort box was redrawn with the new model's levels while the composer still held the old word, so a queued run went out as `--model gemini-3.1-pro --effort medium` and died before it started, in an archive file nobody reads for hours. Model and effort are now narrowed together — page-side and host-side, through one function — and an effort a model does not offer becomes that model's own default, or no flag at all. (X-30)

**Ten items, six surfaces.** The Console's cards carry the session id again beside the agent name, and the card makes room for it rather than hiding it (K-13). The backlog Capture's ↺ puts the box's dragged height back along with the five boxes — the words are still untouched (U-41). The counts strip is a control: press it and a pile with families in it is counted as wholes instead of lines, with the area chips following the same rule so two adjacent lines can never disagree (M-5), and the family filter gained the row that reading needs — *wholes only, nobody's part* (M-6). The "part of" picker's hover panel leaves the scrolling menu that was clipping it, opens against the whole picker at full size, and takes its time both opening and closing so it can be reached (U-43). Launch profiles can be renamed, replaced and deleted from their own rows in the session launcher, and from the bridge (`launch.profiles`, `launch.profile.set` / `.rename` / `.delete`) — a list you could only add to was a list that only grew (A-11).

Verified with 8,125 automated assertions and real Chromium renders of the Console and Projects/Backlog in dark and light themes.

## 3.112.0 — The user's queue and every engine keep their own identity (2026-08-12)

**“Top five” now means the first five in Queue order — with no hidden size ceiling.** `projects.backlog.next` silently defaulted to `maxEffort: M`, so an L/XL item the user had explicitly placed fifth was skipped for a later small item. Effort is now a filter only when the caller explicitly supplies `maxEffort`; the default result is the exact unblocked order shown by **Queue order (yours)** and **Next up**. The bridge contract and both guide languages state the same rule.

**External-engine session pages now parse the engine that wrote them.** A Codex rollout is no longer fed to the Claude parser or identified by its `rollout-…` filename: its real thread UUID, clean user prompt, dialogue, tool calls/results, model, effort, token use and context reach the Session Viewer. Resume resolves external rows too, restores their visible history, keeps their engine/model, and never sends their thread id to `claude --resume`; Claude-only `/context` capture is not offered on those pages. Antigravity exposes no schema/export route for its historical protobuf store, so conversations made before this release cannot be reconstructed. Agent Farm now preserves every Antigravity conversation it hosts as a versioned plain-JSON transcript, making all future dialogue and tools readable after VS Code closes.

**The Console's engine, model and effort boxes are one dependent control.** Changing engine immediately rebuilds the model list from the per-window catalog cache and reconciles effort; changing model immediately rebuilds that model's effort vocabulary and default. Antigravity's remote catalog is fetched once per VS Code process and reused until restart. Opening **Talk to Agent** is a fresh composer even when the Console is already open: it reseeds engine/model/effort/permissions from the current launch defaults instead of inheriting another unstarted session's stale boxes.

**Track opens a transcript to read it, not at an arbitrary event.** Every session/turn door on Project → Track now opens (or resets an already-open viewer) at the page top with **Dialog**, **Collapse all**, **Single-open**, no selected turn and no focused step. Timeline/Search deep links retain their exact-message behavior; this reset belongs only to Track.

Verified with the complete automated suite plus real Chromium renders of the changed Console, Session Viewer and Project Track surfaces in dark and light themes.

## 3.111.0 — Six small doors stop wasting space and clicks (2026-08-11)

**The Projects tab row fits more of the project.** `Overview` is now the shorter `Home`, while Settings is the same neutral tab reduced to its `⚙` icon with an accessibility name and its full meaning in the hover. The ids and navigation contract do not change. On the Backlog toolbar, **Tidy file order now comes before Gather families**, matching the order in which the two ideas are read: repair the document first, then group its queue families.

**A Codex model no longer loses the only word that distinguishes it.** The live Console strip used the shared family/version abbreviation, turning `gpt-5.6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-terra` and `gpt-5.6-sol` into the identical `gpt 5.6`. Codex ids now keep their full readable discriminator (`gpt 5.6 terra · low`); an unfamiliar future suffix is kept too. Claude and Gemini retain their compact family labels.

**Two existing destinations become one-click doors.** Every Notices row now ends with the same subdued `✕` gesture as Needs You; it dismisses that row without opening it or changing its read state. On Project → Track, the `session#turn` closing stamp is now a real button into that exact transcript moment. A legacy stamp with no transcript stays visibly non-clickable instead of pointing nowhere.

**Closed backlog work can age before it disappears.** `Archive all closed` preserves the original sweep, while an adjacent day field + `Archive older` handles 7, 17, 30, 360 or any other whole-day threshold. The age comes from the track ledger's exact close time, then the Tasks plugin's `✅` day. A closed item with no recorded date is deliberately left in place and reported — unknown is never guessed to mean old. The same capability is on `projects.backlog.archive` as `olderThanDays`; invalid and fractional thresholds are refused.

Verified with 8,049 automated assertions and real Chromium renders of Projects/Backlog, Project/Track, Console and Notices in dark and light themes.

## 3.110.0 — Four engines become one readable surface, and a failed turn says why (2026-08-11)

**‼️ THE SESSIONS TABLE WAS STILL A CLAUDE TABLE WITH A GENERAL NAME.** Codex, Gemini and Antigravity conversations now reach the Dashboard, the Sessions page, agent pages and project pages alongside Claude, while the analytic totals stay Claude-only where foreign transcripts do not carry comparable figures. The shared table gained an **Engines** multi-filter derived from the rows it actually holds; an empty engine id correctly means Claude. Every engine uses the same badge component, and Antigravity is red rather than the green Agent Farm reserves for healthy/live state.

**Long project names stop at the Project column.** The project mark now takes at most the width of its cell and hands the shrink to its name, so it ellipsises instead of drawing across Title. Agent gives a quarter of its width back to Title without changing the row's total width. A browser-rendered fixture now keeps four engines and deliberately overlong Turkish project text on this layout, dark and light; it exposed and fixed a stray CSS comment terminator that source-string tests could not see.

**‼️ ANTIGRAVITY'S “DEFAULT” EFFORT NOW NAMES AND SENDS A REAL VALUE.** Models whose catalog rows are split into `low` / `medium` / `high` cannot run as a bare base model: the CLI refuses `--model gemini-3.5-flash` without `--effort`. Their first choice is now `Default → medium` when medium exists, otherwise the cheapest available level, and the exact value goes on the command line. A failed engine turn also keeps the engine's own error sentence instead of being flattened into “turn interrupted”; a missing session id explains whether the engine has not named the conversation yet. Verified against a live Gemini 3.5 Flash turn.

**Plan usage is one compact engine axis everywhere.** The status bar is a strip again (`time left - % / wk %`) with the selected engine and every other engine's figures in its hover. The Dashboard card keeps all four tabs on one line, colours the active engine from the shared palette, switches immediately, and shows the other engines' headline figures below the bars without inventing `0%` for a quota that is not published. The hover has one shape across engines.

**“File under…” now explains the bulk operation it performs.** It stamps the ticked notices — or all visible notices when none are ticked — with the project that places them on that project's Work tab, timeline and filters; the reverse option removes the stamp. The picker sits between Needs You and Export MD, uses the surrounding controls' type size, stays at a measured 169 px instead of growing with the longest project name, and carries the full project list and explanation in its rich hover.

## 3.109.0 — Faz 7: a project has parts, a shape, a letter, and somewhere to go (2026-08-11)

The last phase of the project axis, and all four of its pieces answer the same question in different tenses: **what happens to a project that is not one flat thing in one flat list.**

**‼️ SUB-PROJECTS WERE IN THE MODEL AND ON NO SCREEN.** `parentId` has been in `core/projects.ts` since Faz 1, validated one level deep, orphaning rather than deleting its children — and nothing anywhere drew it. A product with two apps was three unrelated rows. Now a part appears **under its parent and in exactly one place**: nested in the sidebar tree, indented in the page's own list, and never a second time in a status group — because the same project in two rows makes anybody counting them wrong. A part carries **its own status on its row** (a finished part inside an active parent is otherwise a lie the heading tells about it), and an **orphan comes back as a top-level row** rather than vanishing, which is the failure nobody reports because nobody sees it.

**‼️ THE PARTS' MONEY RIDES ALONGSIDE, NEVER INSIDE.** Sessions are disjoint, so adding a parent to its parts is arithmetically legal — and doing it in place would still be wrong: the rows would stop summing to the fleet total, and a reader comparing "TalkScribe $12" against the Sessions table would find twelve dollars nobody can locate. So `partsTotals` hands the fold back **separately**, always with the count that makes it readable (`$11.60 total · +$84.15 in 2 parts`), and the hover says out loud that it is not in the figure beside it. One implementation, shared by the page, the sidebar and the bridge. A brief now also tells an agent working inside a part **that it is one** — otherwise it writes its worklog line, its decision and its backlog item into the sibling folder, and nothing about the path says which.

**PROJECT TEMPLATES — AND NOTHING IS SHIPPED IN THE LIST.** A template is a claim about how somebody works and we do not have one; a built-in "Client project" carrying a guess at five plan items is what gets a feature deleted on sight. So the only door in is **Save as Template on a project that already proved itself**, and it is a **multi-select: what you do not tick does not travel**. Identity, code roots, the agent rule, budgets, people, the folder skeleton, the plan's open items, the queued backlog items — each opt-in, so a template can never surprise you two projects later with a budget you did not choose to carry. **An id, a folder and a parent never travel** (they are identity, not shape) and **neither does the status** — a template taken off a finished project would create everything `done`. On the create form it is a picker whose line says what the chosen one carries, and **anything you type beats it**, because a field that silently reverts to a preset is a form nobody trusts twice. With none saved the control is a **sentence saying where templates come from**, not an empty dropdown, which reads as broken.

**THE HANDOVER — the letter to whoever picks this up in three months, including you.** Not a second report: `projects.report` answers *what happened lately*, this answers *I know nothing about this project and I work on it tomorrow*. It has no period at all. What it is (the charter's own words) · where every document and code root is, with the ones **not on this machine** called out · who to ask · where it stands · what is open and what is **blocked** · **‼️ what somebody already fought and left OPEN**, out of the track ledger with their last note attached — the most expensive thing in any project to rediscover · why it is built this way (its decisions) · what has shipped · how it is actually worked, so you budget your first item against the real median · and a numbered **first five minutes** that names the actual codes. **‼️ The most valuable section is the one machines cannot write**, so the document ends in a block that is yours — the trap, the thing deliberately abandoned, the person who must be told before a release — and **regenerating preserves it verbatim**. That merge is the whole reason the button can WRITE rather than preview; a document that has lost its marker is **refused rather than rewritten**.

**ARCHIVE MIRRORING — a finished project does not vanish, it moves to its counterpart.** The vault has worked this way for years (`10_REPORT` → `99_ARCHIVE/10_REPORT_ARCHIVE`); projects were the one axis with nowhere to go, so the list slowly became a graveyard nobody dared prune. The root is derived from that same convention or set outright (`agentFarm.projectsArchiveRoot`). **‼️ It moves a year of somebody's notes, so it never happens by itself:** setting a project's status to `archived` does **not** touch the disk — "I am done with this" and "move my files" are different sentences and only one of them is undone by clicking again. The confirmation is **modal and prints both paths**; the folder **keeps its name** (every vault link points at it); it **refuses to land on anything that already exists**; the registry **follows the move**; a cross-volume archive falls back to copy-then-remove, and a copy that succeeded with a removal that did not is reported as success with a warning rather than sending you after work already done. **Restore is the exact inverse.** `projects.archived` reads the archive **folder**, not the registry — a project deleted from the registry still has its folder, and that folder is exactly what somebody goes looking for two years later.

Six new bridge ops — `projects.handover` · `projects.templates` · `projects.template.save` · `projects.template.delete` · `projects.archive` (which describes the move and touches nothing until `apply: true`) · `projects.archived` — plus `template` and `parentId` on `projects.create`, and `parent` / `children` / `parts` / `archived` on `projects.get`.

## 3.108.0 — Faz 6: the project proposes, folds itself twice, and tells you about it (2026-08-11)

**‼️ FILING TWO HUNDRED LOOSE SESSIONS IS WORK NOBODY DOES.** A farm that ran for months before projects existed has hundreds of sessions in `Unassigned`. `core/projectSuggest.ts` proposes; a person approves — and **every proposal is a fact you can check in one second**, never a score: *wrote 11 of 11 files under `D:\…\muvu_talkScribe`* · *9 other sessions from this exact folder are filed here* · *88% of that agent's filed sessions are in this project*. There IS a score and it decides the order; it is printed nowhere, because "84% confident" is a number a reader cannot check and a list of those is a list they abandon.

Four rules, each the answer to a way this goes wrong. **Only the undecided** — a session the rules already place is settled, and one you explicitly took OFF every project is never proposed for and is counted separately, because an engine that keeps suggesting what a person refused is one they switch off. **The strongest signal is the one no path rule can see**: membership by path asks where a session *stood*, `files` asks what it *touched*, and an agent editing a repo three directories from its own home folder was invisible to every rule. **The folder cohort counts explicit filings only**, or it would be an echo of the rule that placed them — agreeing with itself and calling that evidence. And **‼️ a contested row is reported, never resolved**: two projects within a hair of each other is a question the engine could not answer, so it names both and **"select all" leaves it out**. You can still tick it by hand; a bulk gesture that settled those would file wrong at two hundred times the speed. Applying sends **session ids only** — the host re-derives the project, so a page holding a stale list cannot write one the engine no longer proposes.

**The Analysis tab — the same data folded a second time.** Weeks · the **rhythm** of when work actually happens by local weekday and hour · **cycle time** from an item's first recorded touch to its close · where the writing lands · a recent **pace against the project's own average**. Three rules, and each is a way an analysis page lies: **a figure with no denominator worth dividing by is not printed** (rhythm needs 40 turns, cycle time 5 closed items, pace 2 weeks — below that the page says *what* is short and *how much*, because a shape read off six points is one a reader will believe); **a trend is stated against a control**; and **local days, Monday weeks** — a session at 23:40 belongs to that evening, and a quiet week is a zero row, never a gap. The backlog side comes from the **track ledger**: a `[x]` says an item *is* closed, only the ledger says *when*, and reading the file would date every item to the last time somebody saved it in Obsidian.

**The Code tab — two sources, never summed.** Commits are what git records in a declared root: everything anybody typed there. `sessionWrites` is what this project's own sessions wrote, out of their transcripts, including files outside any repo. A total over both would be a third number that means nothing. A root that is not on this machine is reported **missing**, because an empty commit list there reads as "quiet repo".

**Health reaches the Living page.** The project page always told the truth about missing folders and blown budgets — on the project page, which is where you go when you already suspect something. Living is the screen for when you do not. **One row for all projects**, so nine of them cannot push every other loop off the page, but it **names** them; a missing folder is **dead**, not a warning, because the plan, the backlog and the worklog are inside it. It prints the warnings the project page computed rather than re-deriving them: the same check written twice is two answers that disagree within a week.

**A weekly digest that reaches you.** Off by default (`agentFarm.projectDigestDay`); set a weekday and each open project's week lands on the **Notices board** as a real summary with the full report as its second leg — a report written to a folder nobody opens is the exact failure the Living page exists to make visible. Stamped with its project, so it shows on that project's Work tab too. Once per project per week, guarded by **the board itself** rather than a timestamp of ours: two windows cannot double-post, and one you deleted can come back.

**Two things fixed on the way.** Two full-width bar charts and a heatmap turned Analysis into a page you scroll past — a chart is capped now, and a bar list of two rows no longer scales one author's name larger than the page title. And the backlog flow said `net −7`, which could mean seven fewer items or seven fewer closures; it says **"7 fewer open"**.

**On the bridge:** `projects.suggest` · `projects.suggest.apply` · `projects.analysis` · `projects.code` · `projects.report` (the same document over any period, `post:true` to put it on the board).

## 3.107.0 — Faz 5: a project runs work now, and every piece of it carries a stamp (2026-08-11)

**‼️ THE QUESTION A PROJECT PAGE EXISTS TO ANSWER HAD NO ANSWER ON IT.** A project could say what had *happened* (Timeline) and what it had *cost*. The work itself lived on five other pages, each filtered by hand — so *what is this project actually doing* was a question you answered by opening five tabs and remembering which filter you had set on each.

**Four models, one field.** Tasks already carried a `projectId`; **missions, notices and work-queue items** carry one now, and a mission's project reaches **every delegation its planner makes** — a wave fires whenever the planner decides to, which may be an hour after anybody looked at a window, so reading the window there would have shown the mission and none of the work it caused.

**Nothing is derived, and that is the design rather than a shortcut.** The stamp is set by whoever makes the thing: the window's active project when a **person** made it, an explicit `project` argument when an agent did, a **declaration on the automation** when nobody was at a window at all. Inferring a notice's project from who posted it would file half of them wrong — an agent works on several projects — so unstamped work is simply absent from a project, and every surface says so instead of looking empty. An unknown project name is **reported back** (`projectError`) rather than swallowed: a misspelt name used to be indistinguishable from no project, which is how an agent could believe for a month that its reports were reaching a page they had never touched.

**A mark you cannot take off is a mark nobody trusts** — which is the only reason automatic stamping is safe. The queue's capture box **shows** what it is about to file under and lets you change it before Add; every notice and every queue item has a project picker on its own row whose empty option clears the stamp; both boards do it in bulk over the selection or over everything the filter shows. Deleting a project prunes the stamps that pointed at it (a task's is left alone — it is a record of what a finished run *ran with*, not an assignment).

**Both boards gained a project filter, and "No project" is a real option.** Filtering by project must never make the unfiled rows vanish with nothing on screen saying they exist — they are exactly the ones you are about to want to file. A filter naming a project nothing matches returns **nothing**, not everything: a filter that silently stops filtering hands back the whole board and looks like an answer.

**The Work tab** groups all five by how much they need you — *Running now* · *Needs you* · *Queued* · *On the books* · *Fires by itself* · *Settled* — and an empty group is **not drawn**, because five cards each saying "nothing here" is five paragraphs above the one that has something. A tab with nothing at all says why and what to do. Rows carry what their own page would say: a queued task shows the **gate's own words** for why it has not started; a mission says whether it was *started in* this project or is here because its *delegations* were; a schedule shows when it fires and whether it has a brake.

**And work starts here.** *Start a session here* makes the project active **first** — that is what puts its brief into the agent's system prompt at spawn — then opens the Console composer, or a terminal. Beside it: a new task, schedule or trigger, each opening the **same form** every other route opens with the project filled in. The tab writes to none of the five stores itself; it forwards to whatever already owns them.

**Two things this fixed on the way.** "Open" on a task or mission row passed an id to a command that takes no arguments, so a timeline row opened a list of two hundred and left you to find the one you had just clicked — both panels take a focus now. And the project page's relative clock printed `-27304s ago` for a timestamp a few seconds ahead of this machine's, which is normal and is the page calling itself broken.

**One more component, for the reason `afEngine.js` exists:** `media/afProject.js`, so the project mark (its own colour, its icon, its hover) is drawn the same way on the sessions table, the notices board, the queue and anywhere the next surface needs it — the dot used to live in `sessionsTable.js`'s own stylesheet, which is how a second page would have invented a second one.

**On the bridge:** `projects.work` (the same five stores, `open:true` for only what is unfinished) · `notices.project` · `queue.project` · and `project` on `notices.post`, `notices.list`, `queue.add`, `queue.list` and `missions.start`.

## 3.106.0 — A chunk boundary is not a character boundary, and two more the new engines exposed (2026-08-11)

**‼️ U-36 — `hazır<?><?>m.` WAS NOT THE ENGINE. IT WAS THIS READER.** Talking to Antigravity in Turkish produced replacement characters in the middle of words, and the diagnosis relayed from the agent itself was right: the text is cut at a transport boundary. The cut was here. `child.stdout.on('data', d => String(d))` decodes **each chunk on its own**, and a chunk ends wherever the pipe happened to flush — which on a Turkish reply lands inside a two-byte `ü` / `ı` / `ş` often enough to see. Each half decodes to U+FFFD. The engine sent correct UTF-8; the reader sliced it.

The fix is a held decoder — `StringDecoder`, **one per child**, which keeps an incomplete trailing sequence back until the next chunk completes it (a shared one would splice two conversations together at a boundary), plus a flush on `end` so a truncated stream loses no final line. `test/test_stream_utf8.js` proves both halves against every single split point of a real Turkish payload: the old path corrupts at exactly one point per interior byte of every multi-byte character, the new one at none — and survives a character split across **three** chunks. **It was never engine-specific.** Claude simply got lucky more often, because its stream-json lines are shorter and flush on their own newlines; the same cut was always possible there.

**U-37 — a session on a new engine never got a name, and the cause was one word.** The Console read a session's title out of `index.all()`, which is *deliberately* the Claude-only half — external sessions are kept out of it so Claude's analytics can never see them. So a Codex or Antigravity card looked itself up in a list it is structurally absent from, found nothing, and read *(no name yet)* for ever: it looked like a feature that had not been built for them rather than a lookup in the wrong list. Three layers now, in order — the index (either half), then the conversation itself through `conversationTitle`, the **same** function the transcript scan uses. Which also means a hosted session is named on its first message instead of whenever a transcript happens to be parsed.

**U-38 — the engine badge took the room the session id had.** `agent (#019fe7bc)` plus a badge plus a token figure is three things in a one-column card, and the id was the one being cut — `(#019f…` says nothing at all, which is worse than not drawing it. It is a value you **copy**, never one you read at a glance, so it moved into the row's hover (whole, on both the live cards and the *Where you left off* shelf). The card's token figure is the **raw** count only: the weighted one is the honest answer to "how much of the 5h window did this burn" and it is kept everywhere it can be read properly — the tables, the hover, the Dashboard — but at card width it was a second number nobody could tell from the first. `tok` → `tk`, for the same three characters of room.
## 3.105.0 — The model box was never being answered at all, and the engine axis reaches the last four surfaces (2026-08-11)

**‼️ FIRST, THE BUG UNDER THE BUG (U-31). Choosing Antigravity still offered `haiku / sonnet / opus / fable` — and it was not a refresh problem, it was a handshake.** `agy models` **does not exit while its stdin is open.** Measured on this machine, same binary, four ways: `execFile` with stdin left open → 20 000 ms, SIGTERM, **zero bytes**; through a shell → the same; `execFile` + `stdin.end()` → 3 892 ms, eleven rows; full path + `stdin.end()` → 3 954 ms, eleven rows. So every fetch this extension had ever made was killed by its own timeout with nothing to show, the cached catalog never became anything, and the picker fell back to four Claude aliases **permanently** — on an engine that rejects all four. One line fixes it. The other half was real too: the answer arrives ~4 s after the panel drew and **nothing told the panel**, so the open pages are now redrawn when the list changes, the catalog is warmed once eight seconds after activation, and `engines.models { refresh: true }` is finally *awaited* instead of returning the list it was refreshing away from.

**‼️ AND THE SAME MODEL WAS LISTED TWICE (U-32).** `fable` and `Fable` were the user's alias and the CLI's own `claude-fable-5[1m]` — one model, two rows, differing by a capital letter, because deduplication compared `id` against `id`. Identity is the **family** now: the vendor prefix, the version tail and the bracketed variant come off, so `claude-fable-5[1m]`, `claude-fable-5` and `fable` all reduce to `fable`. The alias keeps the id (it is the name that goes on resolving to the latest model), the catalog lends its label and description, and a row that really is a variant is drawn **as** one — `Fable · 1m` — so nothing is lost and nothing reads as a duplicate.

**U-26 — which engine ran it, everywhere, and Claude wears a badge too.** The old rule was "the default wears nothing", which left an unbadged row meaning either *Claude* or *this surface never learned about engines* with nothing to tell them apart. Every engine has one badge and one fixed colour now, from one component (`media/afEngine.js`) — so the Console, the sessions tables and a session's own page cannot disagree about what a Codex row looks like. The tables gained an **Engine** column, Agent is half again as wide (it could not hold a name), and Project keeps a quarter of what it was taking.

**‼️ …and Antigravity had never appeared in a sessions table AT ALL.** An Engine column that can never print one of the four engines is a claim, not a column. Its own transcripts cannot be read here and that is worth saying rather than working around: each conversation is a SQLite db whose `steps` table holds protobuf blobs with no schema on disk. What IS readable is the CLI's index of them, which carries exactly what a row needs — the id, a title, the step count, the time and the **workspace**, which is what attributes a conversation to an agent. Seven real conversations appeared the moment it was wired.

**U-27 — the remaining-limit indicator was answering for one engine and labelled for all of them.** It read Anthropic's usage endpoint and printed `3h 49m - 86% / wk 41%`, which is true, and true about Claude only. The axis is the engine now: the status-bar hover lists all four **with each one's figure and a link to switch**, the Dashboard card grew mini tabs, and both write the same setting so they cannot drift. **‼️ Codex's quota was on disk and nobody had looked** — every rollout transcript records the `rate_limits` block the service returned at each turn boundary, so that figure costs nothing and asks nothing (it is as old as the last turn, and the card says so instead of claiming a beat it does not run). **Antigravity reports `not published`, never 0%**: its CLI refreshes a quota internally and writes it to no file, and a bar reading zero for an engine nobody can measure is the most dangerous thing that surface could draw.

**U-29 / U-33 — "Default" now says what it resolves to, per engine.** The complaint was exact: *I may have set that agent's default to sonnet for Claude, but if I open the same agent with Codex, what does Default still mean there?* It meant nothing — the one per-agent default was written to `<agent>/.claude/settings.local.json`, a file **only Claude Code reads**, so the same box on a Codex row was a choice that went nowhere. Farm Settings → Agent folders now carries an engine strip, each agent has a model **and** an effort box per engine drawn from **that engine's own catalog**, and every box names the value it will actually produce and who decided it: the agent's own file · Agent Farm's per-engine store (passed as a flag, so a terminal session will not have it) · the fleet-wide `launchDefaults` · the CLI. The launcher reads the same resolution, so the sentence the box prints and the value the session gets cannot disagree.

**U-28 — the composer row is a row again.** It was `flex-wrap:wrap` over selects with no declared width, so a long project name grew its own box and pushed **Send** onto a second line: the control you reach for moved because of a string in a box next to it. The widths are declared (measured first: agent 121 · engine 145 · model 97 · effort 66 · permission 133 · project 105, then cut by the shares asked for), the row cannot wrap, the send buttons never give up a pixel, and when space is short the boxes shrink in a **chosen order** — project and permission first, the agent last. The transcript also re-pins itself *after* the live strip has taken its height, which is why the last line used to sit one row below the fold when a turn started.

**U-30 — sixty words under the composer became a `?`.** What a per-turn engine changes about talking to it was printed as three lines in the narrowest strip on the page, every draw. Same text, one character wide, in the rich hover panel — and offered for **every** engine, so "no note" can never be mistaken for "none written".

**Two more the sweep turned up.** The Windows scheduled-task script hard-coded `claude -p`: it is what fires when VS Code is closed, so it quietly ran a different engine from the one the same schedule uses inside the farm — it builds the fleet engine's own command line now, and the message names that engine instead of always saying `claude`. And the speaker label read the vendor off the model id, which stopped being safe when one Antigravity account started serving three vendors' models: `gpt-oss-120b` was being called Codex. The session's own engine wins; the id is the fallback.

**A trap the test suite could not see.** An editing tool wrote a literal `U+0000` into a source file where an escape was meant, and it survived everything — `tsc` compiled it, the bundle shipped it, and `grep` quietly reclassified the whole file as binary. `test/test_encoding.js` scans C0 controls now, not just the C1 range. New alongside it: `test/measure_page.js`, which runs a rendered page through the already-installed Chrome and prints what an expression returns, so a column width can be **measured** instead of argued about in shares of a guess.

**On the bridge:** `usage.engines` (every engine's limit state, with the reason wherever there is no figure — never a zero) and `agents.defaults` (what "Default" resolves to for an agent on an engine, with the layer that decided it). `engines.models { refresh: true }` now waits for the answer.
## 3.104.0 — A fourth engine speaks: Antigravity runs in the Console, and it is not the Gemini row (2026-08-10)

**‼️ FIRST, THE NAMING, BECAUSE THE CONFUSION WAS THE ITEM (S-21).** The `gemini` row is `@google/gemini-cli`, a separate program that **is not installed on this machine**. Antigravity is Google's agent app, its CLI is `agy`, and the models it offers are Gemini's *and* Anthropic's *and* an open-weights one — so calling it "Gemini" would have been wrong in both directions at once. The engine is **Antigravity**, its id is `agy`, and it is the fourth engine rather than a replacement for anything.

**Adding an engine cost two files, which was the point of the shape.** `core/agy.ts` (its command line) and `core/agyproto.ts` (its stream, translated) — the panel, the state, the cost strip, the step icons and the transcript are the ones that were already there. Not one field name is shared with Claude's `stream-json` or with Codex's; `agy` puts text, thinking, tool calls, tool output and the token count on ONE event type (`init` · `step_update` · `result`).

**Everything below was measured against agy 1.1.11 on this machine, signed in through the Antigravity app with no API key anywhere. Five of the measurements changed the design:**

**‼️ `--add-dir` IS NOT OPTIONAL.** The process's cwd is reported back in the `init` event and then **ignored**: the agent works in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/scratch` instead. Measured — asked for `package.json` in a repo, it looked in the scratch folder and answered "the file does not exist"; with the folder handed to it, the same question returned the real version string. **An engine that silently answers about the wrong folder is worse than one that refuses**, so every surface that starts a session on it passes the directory: the Console, the terminal launcher (its preview line included, or it would show one command and run another) and an unattended task.

**‼️ THE PROMPT TRAVELS IN ARGV, NOT ON STDIN — the opposite of Codex.** Measured: `printf … | agy -p ""` exits 1 with *empty prompt. Usage: agy --print "your prompt here"*. Which means the process is spawned with **no shell** — a multi-line message does not survive `cmd.exe` — and `agy` being a real `.exe` is what makes that possible. Verified with a prompt carrying newlines, double quotes, a backtick, `$`, `%` and `&`: it arrived intact. Where the prompt goes and whether a shell may see the command line are now fields on the transport rather than habits at the call sites (`promptOn`, `spawnShell`), and the per-turn vocabularies are dispatched in one place instead of by name in three.

**‼️ A TERMINAL CAN GENUINELY RESUME THIS ONE.** `--conversation <id>` is an ordinary flag on the ordinary command, measured to come back with the same id and the context carried. Codex is refused there because its resume is a subcommand with a different flag vocabulary that nobody here has measured — so the rule now follows each engine's own vocabulary (`resumeInTerminal`) instead of "is it Claude".

**‼️ ITS MODEL LIST IS THE ONLY ONE THAT IS NOT ON DISK.** `agy models` asks the service and prints `id<TAB>label` rows to stdout (the banner goes to stderr) in ~3.4 s, costing **no tokens** — it is a list, not a turn. So it is fetched in the background, kept with the time it arrived, and re-asked with `engines.models { refresh: true }`. Eight of its eleven rows are three models at three efforts, and offering all eleven would have left the effort box dead **and** built a command line the CLI refuses (*invalid model selection … conflicts with --effort=medium*). They are split back into base × effort — and the CLI's own error message is the cross-check: *gemini-3.1-pro has no "medium" effort (available: low, high)*, which the parser derives from the list alone. A model with no effort offers an empty box rather than the generic one.

**‼️ ITS PERMISSION MODE IS NOT A GUARANTEE, AND NO PICKER PRETENDS OTHERWISE.** `--mode plan` was measured with "create a file containing WROTE" — **and the file was created**. The `init` event reports `permission_mode: always-proceed` whatever is passed, because the governing setting is the Antigravity app's own `toolPermission`. The flag is still sent (it is the engine's vocabulary and it steers the agent) but every surface says where the real restraint lives, and a task archive on this engine says *Permissions*, not *Sandbox*.

**Two counting traps, both the inverse of Codex's.** `result.usage` is the **sum** of the steps' usage — measured: 17456+117+5385+5627+5760+2413 = 36758, and the result reports exactly 36758 — so the steps are counted and the result is not, or every turn would double. And `input_tokens` does **not** include the cached share here (Claude's arrangement, not Codex's), so applying Codex's subtraction would have undercounted rather than fixed anything. `thinking_tokens` is inside `output_tokens`, not beside it.

**‼️ AND ITS SIGN-IN IS NOT A FILE.** No `~/.agy`, no `~/.antigravity`, nothing under `~/.gemini` naming a token, nothing in the Windows credential store, and no `agy login` command: the grant belongs to the Antigravity **desktop app** and the CLI borrows it. What is on disk is the CLI's own note that onboarding finished, so that is what the row reads — and it says where the grant actually lives instead of naming a file that has none. This is the first engine with **no key route at all**, which is a real state and not a missing one: `key.env` is empty, the "Add key" button is not drawn, and the row's next step is a sentence rather than "Put  in the vault".

**Proof, not assertion.** `test/smoke_agy_console.mjs` drives the real `ConsoleHost` against the real engine: two turns, the same conversation, context carried, a file read in the session's own folder, a multi-line prompt a shell would have destroyed, tokens counted, no invented price. The ⚡ Test button runs one real turn on the catalog's cheapest model and answered in 9.7 s.

## 3.103.0 — The model box asks the engine instead of a constant, and the second leg stops failing quietly (2026-08-10)

**‼️ THE ENGINE AXIS ARRIVED AND THE BOX BESIDE IT DID NOT (S-20).** Whichever engine was chosen, the model list was `agentFarm.modelAliases` — four Claude aliases typed by hand. Picking **Codex** still offered `sonnet`: a `--model` its CLI can only reject, while its own `/model` was offering six others. The same root rots the Claude half on a slower clock — **a hardcoded list is an expiry date**, since a constant only changes when somebody remembers it exists. So the list is no longer written, it is READ (`core/models.ts`), per engine, on every call: Codex from `~/.codex/models_cache.json` (the catalog its CLI refreshes against the service — filtered by `visibility` and sorted by `priority`, which reproduces the terminal's six rows in the terminal's order, routing aliases and internal review models excluded), Claude from its aliases **plus** `additionalModelOptionsCache`, what this account was additionally granted, so a newly granted model appears without an edit.

**Effort belongs to the model, not to a list.** The Codex catalog states each model's own reasoning vocabulary — six levels on terra including `ultra`, four on 5.5 — with the service's own words on each. One shared `EFFORT_CHOICES` was wrong for both, so the effort box now narrows to the chosen model, and the engine and model boxes redraw the form because they decide which options exist.

**A fallback that cannot be mistaken for an answer.** When a catalog cannot be read — Codex installed but never run — the aliases stand in, but `source` says `settings` and the note says what is being shown is not the engine's own answer. Every box carries where its list came from and how old it is: a list nobody can date is a list nobody can distrust. **Changing the engine drops a model it never had**, along with the effort chosen for it. On the bridge in the same commit: `engines.models` — models per engine, efforts per model, and which of the two the answer was.

**‼️ AND A REPORT ASKED FOR BUT NOT WRITTEN USED TO ANSWER `ok: true`.** Measured: a 14 KB user guide posted through `notices.post` produced a notice, no file and no word about it — the caller believed it had delivered. Two causes, both closed. The bridge now undoes PowerShell's silent string→object mangling (`Get-Content -Raw` returns a *decorated* object, so `ConvertTo-Json` emits `{value, Length, PSPath}` instead of a string, and only the LONG argument is ever affected), and the second leg reports what happened to it: `notices.post` and `queue.stamp` answer with `reportError` / `answerError` instead of swallowing an unusable body or a failed write.

## 3.102.0 — Every button that starts something asks which engine, and a plan-billed run stops pretending to have a price (2026-08-10)
