They do not: the vault's own rule is that Turkish characters in file names are preserved, and these names exist to be read by a person in a folder listing. Ids, git branches and HTML anchors keep folding, as they must.

## 3.86.0 — A search box answers the question you asked, a dotted İ is a letter again, and the box you drag stays dragged (2026-08-08)

**‼️ PRESSING `🧑 User` ON THE SEARCH PAGE TURNED IT OFF (U-3).** The kind chips were a plain toggle, and the default scope is Dialog — you + the agent — so the two chips a reader most wants to press were **already lit**. Pressing `🧑 User` therefore *removed* it and left the search running over agent replies alone: the chip you pressed went dark, the one you did not touch stayed lit, and the row read as if the click had not registered. It had; it had done the opposite of what it looked like. **A plain click now means "only this kind"** and Ctrl/⌘-click keeps the old add-and-remove — the same two gestures the Console's `show` box learned in 3.84.0, one vocabulary across the extension. That also makes the two questions this page exists for one press each: *only what I asked* and *only what it answered*. The row says in words which kinds it is searching (`→ the conversation`), so "which one is on" never means reading four chips at once.

**And the page gained the filters the Sessions and Turns tables already had.** A **When** range — and it filters **what was said**, the message's own time, not the session's last-touched date, so "the 1st" means that day's words and not every transcript that happens to have been written to since. It applies to **all three sources**: transcripts, the agents' memory notes and the orphaned live events. A filter that quietly governed one third of the results would be one nobody could use. Results can be **ordered** — newest, oldest, most matches, by agent — and only the session *groups* move: hits inside a session stay in the order they were said, because the turn sub-headers are computed by walking that order and any other would make them a lie. **Clear filters** puts the three back and leaves your query and your agent picker alone; it is drawn inert while there is nothing to clear rather than pretending. The memory/events ticks are held in the page's state now — `shell()` rebuilds the markup on every chip press, and a checkbox written as `checked` came back ticked however you had left it.

**‼️ AND THE SEARCH PAGE'S OWN QUESTION IS ON THE BRIDGE (`search.transcripts`).** An agent's commonest research question — *where did we discuss this, and what was said* — had no answer there at all, so the only route was reading transcripts by hand while the panel that answers it in a second sat behind a webview. Same engine, same query language, same three sources: `query`, `roles` (`["user"]` = only what a person asked), `from`/`to` (ISO or ms), `agents`, `file`, `memory`, `events`, `limit`. Every hit carries the `file` + `ts` that `sessions.open` takes to land on the exact moment — and the result **says when the scan was cut short**, because an agent that does not know a read was partial will report "nothing was ever said about X".

**‼️ A SEARCH RESULT NOW OPENS IN THE SCOPE THE SEARCH WAS MADE IN (S-4).** Click a hit found in Dialog and the transcript opened showing **everything** — every tool call and result in front of somebody who had said, one click earlier, that they were reading the conversation. And it was not the deep link's doing: a prompt and the tool call it sets off **share a timestamp**, the jump picked whichever came first in the file, and reaching a target the filter hid meant wiping the filter. So the tie is broken the other way — **a message already on screen at the same instant wins** — and the Search page hands over what it was searching in, so a Dialog search opens in Dialog and an Everything search opens in everything. A target genuinely reachable only by widening still widens: a jump that lands nowhere is worse than a view showing more than you asked for.

**‼️ THE WRITING BOX STAYS THE HEIGHT YOU DRAGGED IT TO (K-9).** Two separate things threw it away, which is why it looked random. The ceiling was `30vh`, so a drag past a third of the window did nothing and the handle stopped under the cursor. And the height a drag sets is an **inline style on the element**, which `buildShell` replaces — so any rebuild (another session picked, a turn ending, an agent pinned) put the box back to 64px, and whether your drag survived depended on whether a rebuild happened to land between it and your next look. The ceiling is 70vh (the conversation above is a `min-height:0` scroller and simply yields the room), and the height is **saved with the draft it holds** — it outlives a rebuild, a session change and the panel being closed. A window resize is not mistaken for a drag: the inline value is what is watched, and a re-layout does not touch it.

**‼️ THE CALENDAR STOPS SHUTTING ITSELF BEFORE YOU PICK A DAY (A-4).** Open a date filter and it could close under the moving cursor — sometimes. One cause: the filter applied **as you type**, and applying repaints the page, and a repaint replaces the element the native calendar popup is hanging off. `<input type="date">` fires `input` whenever its three segments happen to form a valid date, which during a picker gesture is while you are still moving through it — so whether it survived depended on where in the popup you had got to and whether the field already had a value. Date boxes commit on **`change`** only now, which is the event that means *a date was chosen* and fires after the popup has closed itself: nothing is lost, and the gesture is never interrupted. One fix in the shared control (`media/afFilters.js`), so every date filter in the extension has it.

**‼️ A DOTTED İ IS A LETTER, NOT A LETTER PLUS A SEPARATOR (X-9).** A decision titled *"İş türü kararı"* filed itself as `2026-08-05-i-ş-türü-kararı`. `'İ'.toLowerCase()` is `'i' + U+0307 COMBINING DOT ABOVE` — the fold splits the letter into a letter **plus a mark**, and a mark is neither `\p{L}` nor `\p{N}`, so every separator rule in the codebase turned it into a separator. Measured, there were **three different answers** to one question across four call sites: the decision file name broke on İ; the guide anchors and duty slugs were right for Turkish but deleted `Â`/`é`/`î` outright (`Âlem Beyoğlu Café` → `lem-beyoglu-caf`); and production ids and git branch names turned **every** non-ASCII letter into a dash (`i-t-r-karar`). One function now (`slugify`), with two audiences: names a **person** reads keep their alphabet, because the vault's own rule is that Turkish characters in file names are preserved; **ids and branches** fold to ASCII by mapping the six Turkish letters and decomposing the rest, so `Âlem Café` becomes `alem-cafe` instead of losing three letters. Not `toLocaleLowerCase('tr')`, which would also map plain `I` to `ı` and mangle every English title to fix one Turkish one. And a cut no longer leaves half a word — `…-p-7-k-8-x-7-s` reads as corruption, not brevity.

**‼️ A SESSION BRIEFED THROUGH `/goal` IS NAMED AFTER THE BRIEF (U-17).** `/goal finish the top eight backlog items` reaches the transcript as a command block, and a block starts with `<` — so the whole opening turn was discarded as plumbing. The command was; **the argument was the entire brief.** Sessions opened that way went on to be named by whatever ordinary message came next, which in a long run is a continuation — one was called *"devam et"*, while the sentence saying what the work was about sat in the first turn, thrown away. The argument names the session now, and the shape test is what makes that safe: it is taken only when it reads as **prose**, so `/goal clear`, `/model opus` and `/loop 5m /x` are still machinery. The turn is **not** counted as a prompt — every figure the tables show stays exactly what the transcript says; all that changed is whether it may speak for the session.

**‼️ THE START PICKER OPENS ON THE PROJECT THE STATUS BAR IS SHOWING (U-16).** 3.84.0 made a new session belong to nothing until you said so, which fixed a real thing — a session filed, briefed and charged to a decision nobody made *here*. But the danger in that was **silence, not inheritance**: a window whose status bar names an active project, and which then offers `No project` for the session you start from the tree, is asking you to say the same thing twice. It inherits **and says so**: the row names the project and credits *"the window's active project"*, and the row directly under it — `Choose a different project…  · or no project at all` — changes or drops it in one press, because a statement does not look like a control and "press the line that states the fact" is not something a reader knows to try. Nothing active → `No project`, exactly as before. A closed or archived project is never seeded, and this picker still never *writes* the window's active project. The Console composer is unchanged: it is per-session by design and starts at none.

**↺ on the Backlog's CAPTURE heading (S-14).** The four boxes keep their last answers on purpose — filing a run of items into one area costs one choice instead of one per item — which is exactly why the fifth item, of a different kind, quietly inherited the fourth's area, type, priority and size. A small mark on the heading puts all of them, and the queue place, back to blank; its hover names the values it is about to drop, and what you have **written** is never touched. Inert while there is nothing to reset.

**Shared:** a **✕ inside the box** is a component now (`.af-clr` + `AFFilter.clearable`), born and dying with the text rather than hidden and shown — an empty box with a dead ✕ in it is a control claiming it can do something. On the Search query box (where clearing also clears the results, since hits under an empty box are the page claiming to have found something for nothing) and on the Sessions and Turns text filters.

## 3.85.0 — The backlog is flat again, a hover survives the repaint that was closing it, and an event says which build saw it (2026-08-08)

**‼️ THE BACKLOG IS A FLAT LIST, AND THE HIERARCHY NOBODY ASKED FOR IS GONE (P-9).** An agent filed a parent item with eight children of its own accord — a restructuring that was never agreed to, and one waiting in the backlog as its own item (M-2) to be done properly later. It looked exactly as bad as an unasked-for redesign does: the queue is one flat list of codes, but rows were indented by the *file's* indentation, so in queue order `U-14.1` sat tucked under `U-12` — a child drawn under a stranger — and every re-sort scattered them somewhere new. **An item is an item now:** no sub-items, no epics, no parents, no containers, nowhere. The eight children were folded into their parent as numbered sub-paragraphs with every word kept, and their lines deleted. Gone with them: the indent on backlog and queue rows, the `epics` filter (leaves only / containers only), the bold on a "container", `parent` on `projects.backlog.add` and `.code` and `.promote`, the container rules in `next`/`queueOrder`/`tidy`/`archive`, and the "parent is not in this file" warning — there is no parent to be missing. **A dotted code is still read** (`U-14.3`), because a code is permanent and somebody's file may already hold one, but it is never handed out again and it hangs nothing under anything; `nextCode` counts it under its own first number, so a flat code can never collide with one.

**‼️ A RICH HOVER NO LONGER CLOSES ITSELF WHILE YOU ARE READING IT (U-15).** Rest on a session's Title, start reading the opening prompt, and the panel shut — with the mouse never having moved — and did not come back. Not a timeout: these pages redraw whole sections on the host's own beat, and **a browser does not put `:hover` back on the node that replaced the one the pointer was on** until the pointer moves again. Every surface using the panel had it. The fix is in the component (`media/afTip.js`), not in any page: when the wrapper under the pointer is torn out, whatever is now **where the pointer entered** gets a stand-in class — the panel's scroll position riding along, so a long prompt does not jump back to its first line — and that class lasts exactly until the pointer moves and `:hover` is honest again. Measured against the entry point rather than the current position on purpose: the pointer may be *inside* the panel, which floats over other rows, and restoring from there would open a different row's item. `test_tip_repaint.js` holds it, including the case that must not happen — a repaint nobody was hovering opens nothing.

**The Next up strip hands its hover to that panel too (P-8).** Its chips carried a native `title` cut at 620 characters, which is the wrong treatment for the one thing that makes a code recognisable — a code is three letters; the item behind it is the whole point. Same box the row's `more` opens: the item **whole**, under a head saying where in the queue it is and how big it is, with the figures below it as `Label: value`.

**‼️ "WHAT DID I FINISH LAST?" IS AN ORDERING NOW (P-7).** Bring closed items into view and pick **`closed — last finished first`**: the most recent closing at the top, everything still open at the bottom. The arrow reverses the closed ones and does **not** drag the open ones forward — an item that is not closed is not "closed longest ago". The time comes from the **track ledger** (the moment the box actually moved, to the second, however it was closed) or failing that from the `✅` on the line (the day only); an item with neither is reported with **no** time rather than an invented old one and sits behind the dated ones. Choosing the ordering widens the state filter to include done and cancelled **and says so** — an ordering over a list that hides finished work is an empty answer to a question that was asked properly. Closed rows wear the date, and its hover says which of the two sources it is. Askable from an agent as well: `projects.backlog { sort: "closed" }`, with `closedAt`/`closedOn` on every item.

**‼️ A BACKLOG LINE AN AGENT WROTE SAYS SO (K-8).** Three different things arrive in a backlog and only one of them is a decision its owner made. `projects.backlog.add` now stamps what it writes: **`#by-agent`** — the agent noticed it mid-job and filed it itself, a **proposal** nobody has agreed to — or **`#by-request`** when the user asked for it (`origin:"user"`). The default is `by-agent`, because the mistake worth preventing is the silent one: an agent's own suggestion recorded for ever as the user's decision. An item the user types themselves carries **neither**; absence is the third state. Both are ordinary `#tags` — they filter with everything else and can be added or removed in Obsidian with no tool — and the page only adds a hover saying what they mean, without giving them a work-type colour, because who wrote the line is not what the work is. The rule is in the backlog file's own legend, in the bridge contract, and in the agent-side project procedure.

**‼️ EVERY LIVE EVENT SAYS WHICH AGENT FARM BUILD SAW IT (X-7).** Nothing in any record said which version had been watching, so "capture got worse in 3.58" could only be argued from the **install timestamps of extension folders** — indirect evidence about a directly measurable claim, and one such investigation ran for days. Each line in `live-events.jsonl` now carries an `af` stamp, and each session the build(s) that watched it (`afVersions`, oldest first — more than one means an update or a rollback happened mid-session, and the two halves must not be averaged). **`live.versions`** closes the argument in one pass: per version, when it ran, how many sessions, how many events of each kind, and `perActiveHour` — events per hour **of session activity, not per calendar hour**, because a version that sat installed through a quiet weekend did not capture less. Lines written before the stamp are reported under an empty version as **unknown**, never attributed to whatever was installed at the time.

**‼️ A SESSION IS NAMED AFTER THE CONVERSATION, NOT AFTER A STATE (S-13).** One session was called `[Request interrupted by user]`. The rule that keeps machinery out of titles knew three opening characters — `<` command blocks and local-command output, `/` typed commands, `!` shell passthrough — and a **bracketed notice** is none of them. **The distinction that makes the fix safe:** only a message that is *entirely* one bracket is machinery. `[Image #2] look at this file` is a person talking with an attachment in front of them — measured, a real title in this vault — so the marker comes off and the sentence names the session; a blanket "starts with `[`" rule would have destroyed a good name while fixing a bad one. One function decides (`conversationTitle`) and the session page asks it instead of carrying its own copy — two copies is how the rule drifted apart, and a page disagreeing with the tables about a name is a bug explicable from neither side. The same judgment governs the opening prompt shown on hover, so the two can never disagree. Measured across every transcript on this machine: of 333 named sessions, **2** changed and both improved.

## 3.84.0 — A search box stops asking one question, a new session belongs to nothing until you say so, and a dropdown learns "only this" (2026-08-08)

**‼️ EVERY FILTER BOX IN THE EXTENSION ASKED "DOES THIS SUBSTRING APPEAR?" — AND THAT IS THE WRONG QUESTION THE MOMENT A LIST IS LONGER THAN A SCREEN (P-6).** You could not ask for two words that are not next to each other, nor for either of two things, nor for a whole word rather than a fragment of a longer one, nor to leave something out. Sixteen boxes, one weak question, and the only way through a long list was to squint. There is a **query language** now: `foo bar` means both in any order · `foo OR bar` either (`|` too) · `-foo` / `NOT foo` excludes · `( )` groups · `"a phrase"` is ordered **and whole-word**, so `"log"` is not found inside `dialogue` · `*` is any run of non-space characters · and the two together — `"kelime* bazıları ol*"` finds *"kelimelerin bazıları olsun"* **and** *"kelimenin bazıları oldu"*. **A bare word is still a plain case-insensitive substring**, deliberately: nothing anybody typed before this behaves differently now, and quotes are how you ask for *more* precision rather than less.

**And there is exactly one grammar, because the extension host and the pages run the same file.** `media/afQuery.js` is loaded by every webview and by `src/core/query.ts`, so the transcript search and the box that filters its results cannot drift apart — two query languages wearing the same clothes is worse than one weak one, since the disagreement is invisible. Wired through: the **Search** page, the project **Backlog**, **Sessions** tables, **Turns**, **Tasks** (plan and runs), **Notices** (`#tag` stays its own scope), the **Queue**, **Secrets**, **Toolbox**, **Farm Settings** (three filters), and the Console's **resume filter** and **saved-prompt picker**. `OR`/`AND`/`NOT` are uppercase-only, because "or" is an ordinary word people search for; a hyphen inside a word (`agent-farm`) is a hyphen. The whole-word anchor is Unicode-aware — `\b` is defined on `[A-Za-z0-9_]` and would have failed in the middle of every Turkish word. **A half-typed query is not an error:** these boxes filter as you type, so an unclosed `"` or `(` is treated as the prefix it is instead of blanking the list under your hands. Every box carries the grammar in its own hover with a first line saying what *that* box searches; the guide has the table.

**‼️ A NEW SESSION IS UNDER NO PROJECT UNTIL SOMEBODY SAYS SO (K-3).** The start picker inherited the window's active project, so one choice made in the status bar hours ago, for other work, silently filed and briefed every session started afterwards — and "this one, under nothing" could not be said without changing what the whole window meant. The project question is now **asked first and per launch**: one **`Project`** row above the two places, never the list of names, which lives one step in behind it. Two questions in the order a person thinks them — what is this work, then where do I want to sit while I do it. It starts at `◧ No project` wherever you start from: the QuickPick, the launcher's own Project row, and the Console composer's box. The status bar's active project is still the *window's* answer and neither writes the other. A **resume** is never asked, because `stampSession` would throw the answer away.

**And ▶ → In the Console opens the composer instead of spawning.** Starting the process on the spot put the composer straight into its running shape: the agent fixed (right) and no project box at all (wrong) — while the project rides in on the system prompt *at spawn*, so before the first message is the only moment it can still be chosen, and that was exactly the moment the panel skipped. The agent now comes **pinned** — its name where the picker was, with a ✕ that gives the picker back — and the box stays live. The CLI starts when you send. Nothing is lost: an empty session that was spawned and never typed into was only ever a row to clean up.

**‼️ A DROPDOWN LEARNS "ONLY THIS" (K-1).** Ticking one of seven kinds of line used to mean Clear plus a tick, every time — and before Clear existed, unticking six boxes one at a time. Both gestures are free on **every** multi-pick dropdown in the extension now: the **checkbox** toggles one value as it always did, and the **name beside it means "only this"**. That is why an item is no longer a `<label>` — a label hands every click to its checkbox, which is precisely the behaviour being split in two; the name underlines on hover, because a re-bound click with no affordance is worse than no gesture. Between *Select all* and *Clear* sit the named subsets a particular list has a word for: the Console's `show` box declares **Dialog** (you and the agent, machinery out of the way). The component owns the mechanism, the page owns the word.

**The Console's live strip is two rows (K-10).** On one row the sentence (*"Read src/core/live.ts"*) and every figure the turn had produced competed for the same width, and whichever was longer ate the other — the tail of the meta ran off the right edge and could not be read at all. Row one is **what is happening**; row two is **what it has cost** — duration, money, api calls/steps, held messages, model and effort — right-aligned. The gap between them is deliberately nothing: one strip printed on two rows, not two strips.

**The project chip's hover scrolls (K-2).** The chip exists to show the *whole text* a session is being given — hundreds of lines on a real project. A native tooltip took that, drew as much of it as it felt like, cut, and could be neither scrolled nor reached with the pointer: the one surface built to make an invisible channel visible was the one that could not show what it was hiding. It hangs off the rich panel now (`media/afTip.js`, the box the backlog's `more` proved out).

**🗑 End & Remove Session, on a card's right-click (K-6).** End and Remove stay separate on purpose — a finished conversation must remain readable — but the ordinary case is both, and it cost two right-clicks with a repaint in between that moves the card you were pointing at, so the second one lands on a neighbour. One item does the pair; `console.close { id, forget: true }` was already its agent-side equivalent and now says so.

**Conventions:** `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` gained a **Filtering** section — a search box is a query box (compile once, never `.includes()`), and a multi-pick dropdown has two gestures plus optional presets. `test/lib_afms_dom.js` drives all of it, so any page test can exercise a picker.

## 3.83.0 — A chain is a list, so its form is a list; a retry is an edit; and the last dialogs leave the Tasks page (2026-08-08)

**‼️ THE WIZARD ASKED FOR STEP 4 WITHOUT EVER SHOWING STEPS 1–3 (U-9, phase 2).** `newWorkflow` was **294 lines** of quickpicks and input boxes: it offered to ask for one more step, and there the resemblance to a form ended — no list of what you had already said, no way back to step 2, and Esc discarding the lot. `addWorkflowStep` appended the poorest kind of step the model has (agent · title · prompt · mode, and none of the five things a step actually carries). And `editWorkflowStep` was a **quickpick of field NAMES**: pick "On fail", answer one box, reopen the menu for the next field — fifteen entries deep on a media step, with no way to see two of them at once. The chain is now a list on the page: read it, reorder it with `↑ ↓`, copy a step, delete one, open one in place. **Adding, moving and deleting act on the draft**, so `Cancel` really cancels and a reorder is one save instead of N commands; the save sends the name and the whole chain in one message. **One step is one card, and what it IS decides the card** — 🤖 agent · 🌐 webhook · 🎬 media · 🧭 browser · ✋ approval · 🖼 select, each with its own half and all six sharing the block that is about the *chain* rather than the step (`onFail` · `runIf` · output contract · judge · parallel). In the wizard the kind was the FIRST question and everything after it depended on the answer; in a form you can change your mind on step three. A media step offers the production scene and its vision-QA criteria; a webhook step turns into a submit-then-poll queue client with a checkbox. `agentfarm.newWorkflow`, `addWorkflowStep`, `editWorkflowStep` and `renameWorkflow` all open it — the last one with a blank card, the middle one on that step.

**‼️ AND THE TRIGGER FORM'S "run a workflow" OPTION HAD NEVER BEEN DRAWN.** The page builds that option from `DATA.workflows` and the host has never sent the key, so the list was always empty. Worse than a missing option: editing a trigger that already ran a workflow fell back to the first choice, and the next save quietly turned it into a task trigger. The host sends the list.

**‼️ A RETRY IS AN EDIT, NOT A NEW PROMPT (phase 3).** "Run it again with an edited prompt…" was one input box holding the old words — you could rewrite them and change nothing else, while the commonest reason to retry anything is *the same words on a different model*. It opens the New-task form in retry mode now, whole and prefilled, and the host hands the edits back through `retryTask` so the lineage, the attempt number, the "(retry N)" naming and the workflow-step resume rule stay in one place. **And the retry finally runs the way the original ran:** only `projectId` was ever carried, so a task launched on haiku at low effort came back with no `--model` and no `--effort` at all — the same prompt on a more expensive command line, silently. **"Save the prompt as a template"** opens the template form prefilled instead of a quickpick whose only question was bind-to-agent-or-not; "any agent" is one entry in the picker now rather than a modal asked first. **"Which template?"** is the Plan tab filtered to templates — its rows carry the agent, the mode, the last run and a Run button, which a list of bare titles cannot. **"Which month of history?"** is a list on the page with each archive's size and when it was last written.

**‼️ AND THE GATE IS SET ON THE STRIP THAT REPORTS IT.** 🌙 Quiet called a command that asked a quickpick of two canned windows and then two more input boxes for anything else; pausing asked a duration and then typed the reason into a third. Both are two numbers and a sentence, and they are now a card on the Automation strip. A timed pause **carries its reason** at last — the quick 1h/8h/24h buttons wrote "paused for 480 min" and nothing about why, which is exactly the pause that is still on three days later with nobody able to explain it.

**A workflow's right-click lost two entries and got the right one.** It had "Add a step…" (because Edit could only append one) and "Rename it…" (because that was the only part of a workflow a form could reach). Both are **Edit it…** now, the same entry every other plan row has, and it opens the name and the chain together.

**On the bridge:** `workflows.update` (rename and/or replace the whole chain in one call), `workflows.step.add` · `.update` · `.move` · `.delete`, and `tasks.history`. `tasks.rerun` takes the retry's edits the way the form does — everything omitted is the original's, what you pass is what you are changing. `workflows.step.update` clears the old kind before laying the new step over it: `updateWorkflowStep` merges, and a step's kind is decided by which sub-object it carries, so turning a webhook step into an agent step would otherwise have left the webhook on it and the runner would still call the URL.

**Phase 4 — the sweep, measured.** Of **215** commands, **113** open some native dialog, **58** use `showInputBox`/`showQuickPick`, and **20** are chains of two or more boxes. Thirteen Tasks commands were on that list before this release and none are now. The finding worth keeping is not the count but its shape: **nearly every one of those objects already has a page, and that page lists it without being able to make one** — duties on the agent page, skills and MCP servers in the Toolbox, notes in the memory graph, secrets in the vault, scenes in Studio. The one area with no surface at all is evals and A/B. Filed as backlog `U-14` with eight children, one per surface, because the work costs one commit per surface and twenty per command.

## 3.82.0 — The step counter tells the whole truth, a column is a column on every page, and a schedule stops being a lesser task (2026-08-07)

**‼️ THE NUMBER THAT CALLED ITSELF THE TOTAL WAS MISSING A FIFTH OF ITSELF (S-10, again).** `26api·99steps` reads correctly — two measurements of one turn, the steps carried *by* the calls — but a "step" was defined as *"one reply fragment or one tool call"*, which leaves out the model's own **reasoning**: the kind this extension's own table prices highest and the one it measured happening twice as often as speech. Counted over this machine's six largest recent transcripts: **149 replies + 1 583 tool calls counted, 490 thinking blocks not** — 1 732 reported against a true 2 222, a **22 % under-count of exactly the expensive part**. A step is now any of the three things an agent does: **it reasoned, it wrote, or it called a tool.** A tool *result* is still not one — it is the answer coming back, and results run 1:1 with calls, so counting them doubles the figure. Every turn also carries its `thinking` count, so the hovers break the total into **reasoning / tool calls / replies** with each one's share.

**And the Console stopped working the number out from what it had drawn.** The live strip counted the rows since your last message — which swept in every tool result and every system line the panel writes for itself, and could not see a thinking block at all, because thinking is deliberately never rendered: too big and short of the dearest step at the same time. The count now arrives with the state, off the transcript's own blocks, from the same reducer that counts the API calls. Three surfaces, one definition.

**‼️ A COLUMN'S WIDTH IS DECIDED, NOT INHERITED FROM WHATEVER IS IN IT.** The sessions table laid itself out, which means each column's width is a function of the rows that happen to be listed. Measured in headless Chromium at a 1314px table: with 20 rows Title took **462px**, with 200 rows **457px**, Cost 41px against 48px — so the Dashboard and the Sessions page, the same component twice, could not agree where a column started, and neither could one page after a filter. The shares are now declared (`table-layout: fixed` plus a colgroup) and identical everywhere, with one deliberate change: **Title gives up a quarter of itself** (462 → 346px, re-measured), and the space goes back to every other column in proportion. A text cell shrinks its *words* and never its ⌨/⎋ buttons.

**S-5 closed with it.** The grey dot in front of a project name was the panel border on any project without a colour of its own — an identical dot on every row carrying nothing, in the narrowest column. It is drawn only where there is a colour to draw. The **agent page now has the Project column** it never had, though its filter panel could already filter by project — a filter over a column the reader could not see. And three more places where a native tooltip was carrying TEXT moved to the rich panel: the Console's **saved-prompt picker**, the same list in **Settings**, and a **queue item's own words**.

**‼️ A SCHEDULE IS A TASK THAT HAS NOT HAPPENED YET, AND IT COULD SAY LESS ABOUT ITSELF THAN THE TASK IT MAKES (U-9).** `+ Schedule` opened six to eight VS Code boxes in a row and could not ask **effort, permission mode, project, working folder or priority** — the five dimensions that decide what an unattended run actually does, which matter *more* when nobody is watching. `+ Trigger` was nine boxes. And `+ Template` was worse than missing: the page mapped that button to `runTemplate`, so the ADD button **ran** one, and making a template here was impossible. Schedules and triggers now carry the same run dimensions a task does, and all of it is one form on the page — the same form the toolbar +, a row's Edit, and `agentfarm.addSchedule` / `editSchedule` / `addTrigger` / `editTrigger` / `renameWorkflow` from the palette all open. A schedule's **project is declared on it**, never read from the window: a timer fires at 03:00 with nobody looking. The form redraws when a choice changes what exists (weekly brings a day, watch brings a path, a guard brings its own row) and refuses **in the form**, never in a modal. `triggers.add`, `templates.add` and `templates.list` joined the bridge; `schedules.add` took the new dimensions. Workflow **steps** are the next phase and were left whole rather than replaced by a form that could only name them.

**Sidebar:** the Agents title strip reads left to right and ends with the one button that creates — the Capability Matrix moved to just before **+**, and the Live Timeline (the Live Feed's own button) and the Guide (reachable from the welcome view, the walkthrough and the palette) came off it. **Open Sessions** stopped wearing the Console's glyph: one session is a book, all of them are a library.

## 3.81.0 — The hover stops explaining and starts reporting, the prompt is readable without opening anything, and a scheduled run stops calling itself yours (2026-08-07)

**‼️ A CELL HOVER IS A READOUT, NOT A PARAGRAPH (S-17, again).** The Sessions tables' tooltips had been *filled* — and filled with sentences. Every Duration cell on every row carried *"Wall clock, not time spent working — a session you left open all afternoon is a long one even if it worked for five minutes"*, every Cost cell *"a plan session is not billed this way"*, forever, under a number that needed none of it. A spreadsheet does not annotate its figures; the **column heading** says what a column means, once, and the cells carry figures. So the definitions moved to the `<th>`s, short, and every cell hover is now `Label: value` lines and nothing else.

**And the space that freed went back in as MORE figures, not fewer.** Ctx now says its share of a 200k window, its share of the session's own total, and the per-turn average beside it — it used to say one number and a sentence. Tokens splits in/out/cacheR/cacheW **with each one's percentage** and adds the weight ratio and tokens-per-dollar. Tools names the top four tools *and* the skills and /commands used. Cost derives per turn, per API call, per 1M tokens. Duration gives seconds per API call. **The one exception** is the ⚖ weighted-token note, which hangs off its own `?` and exists to explain — a tooltip whose whole job is teaching is allowed words.

**‼️ …AND A FIGURE THAT NEEDED A DENOMINATOR IT DID NOT HAVE IS GONE.** "Per hour of wall clock" clamped the divisor to one hour, so every session shorter than that reported **its entire cost as its hourly rate** — a twenty-minute $8 session said "$8 per hour" where the arithmetic says $24 and neither number means anything. Sessions under an hour are now given no hourly rate at all.

**‼️ THE TEXT COLUMNS HOVER INTO A PANEL YOU CAN ACTUALLY READ.** A native tooltip repeats what the row already drew and then stops wherever the platform feels like stopping, which is the worst possible treatment of the cells whose content is *words*. **Title** now opens the rich panel the backlog's `more` proved out — scrollable, as wide as it needs, and the pointer can travel into it — carrying the session's identity as `Label: value` and then **the whole opening prompt**, so "what was this session actually asked to do" no longer means opening it. **Project** carries what the project *is*, folders first: docs home, code roots, status, dates, budget, people, id. Inside an expanded row, **every turn's prompt** does the same, whole. The component is `media/afTip.js`, shipped to every page, and `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` now makes it the rule: **anything that is TEXT uses the panel, never `title`.**

**The turn rows carry the detail the session row is a sum of.** A turn's cells now report its API calls, steps, steps per call, tool calls, its top four tools, errors, tokens, weighted tokens, tokens per call, cost, **its share of the session's tokens and cost**, and where it left the window. The Turns page got the same treatment, and its Turn and Session columns the same panel.

**‼️ A SCHEDULED RUN IS NOT YOUR SESSION — even after you have read it.** The origin column (🧑 / 🤖) read the transcript's `entrypoint`, with one exception: any id the Console had ever held counted as a person typing. But that list records every session a Console was asked to **`--resume`** — so opening an automation run in the panel to read it relabelled the run, permanently, as your own dialog. Measured on a real case: a scheduled headless task sat under **🧑 User** while its own limit-retries sat under **🤖 Auto**, which is the same work in two categories. Agent Farm already knows which task/schedule/trigger/mission owns which transcript, so that **recorded fact is now read first** and a run it started is automation whatever happened to it afterwards. The hover — and the session page's subtitle — name the mechanism: *"🤖 schedule · backlog ilerleme"*, not *"automation run"*. `{"op":"sessions.recent"}` returns `launchedBy` and the session `id` with it.

**The session page's own heading is a session row.** Right-clicking it offered Copy/Paste and nothing else — on the one page where there is no doubt which session you mean. It now carries the full session menu, the same one every table row has. And the **session id under it is a button**: one click copies it, and the id itself turns into `✓ copied` for a moment — it is the string most often wanted somewhere else and it was plain text you had to select by hand between two separators.

## 3.80.0 — Seven from the top of the queue: a page that makes tasks instead of opening dialogs, sessions with a page of their own, and two counters that were telling the truth badly (2026-08-07)

**‼️ THE TASKS PAGE MAKES A TASK NOW. IT DOES NOT OPEN A DIALOG THAT DOES (U-9).** The control panel for task activity had no control for making one: **+ Run a task** opened a chain of VS Code input boxes asking title, prompt, mode, CLI, "start now?" — and it could not ask the five that actually decide what an unattended run *does*. A form on the page asks all of them: **agent · prompt · title · mode · model · effort · permission mode · project · CLI · priority · working folder**, with **start it now** as a checkbox rather than a toast you have to catch. The launch dimensions are the launcher's own vocabulary (`core/launch.ts`), so *"run it on opus at low effort"* means the same thing whether you start a session or queue a task — and `permissionMode` matters more here, because a queued run that stops to ask waits until somebody notices it. A task carries `effort` and `permissionMode` through to its command line, headless and interactive alike, and the archived output header says what it was told to be.

**And there is exactly ONE way in.** The sidebar's **+**, the walkthrough and `agentfarm.addTask` all open that same form (prefilled with the agent when you came from an agent row) instead of running a second, shorter set of questions — which is why a task made from the tree and one made from the page used to be different objects. A run's right-click gained **New task like this one…**: the whole task, editable, rather than the old single box that asked for a new prompt and changed nothing else. The duplicate guard is asked *inside* the form, with **Add it anyway** next to the button you just pressed, instead of a modal that takes the focus off the fields it is complaining about.

**`{"op":"tasks.add"}` grew the same dimensions** — `model`, `effort`, `permissionMode`, `cli`, `project`, `priority`, `workDir` — validated the way the launcher validates them, so a typo fails with a readable message instead of the CLI refusing to start inside an archived output nobody reads. `project` follows the usual three states: omitted = inherit the window's, `""` = explicitly none.

**‼️ SESSIONS HAVE A PAGE OF THEIR OWN (S-7).** The table was embedded in two pages and owned by neither: on the Dashboard it is the last block of a board about the whole farm, capped at twenty rows; on an agent's page it is that agent's fifty. *"Show me my sessions"* had no home, and the only way to ask it was **All** on a board that then had to ship every session it had. Same component, same columns, same filters, same right-click contract — **200 rows by default and the whole window**. Three doors: a button beside the Dashboard's **Sessions** heading, an icon in the side panel between **Console** and **Turns**, and links in the status bar's project tooltip that open it **already scoped to that project**. `{"op":"sessions.page","project":"…"}` opens it scoped from an agent.

**‼️ `26api/99` was read as a fraction, and one of the two numbers was counting the wrong thing (S-10).** The arithmetic was right and the string was not: a slash says *"26 out of 99"* to anyone who has not been told otherwise, and the honest reading is neither that nor 26 + 99 — the 99 steps were **carried by** the 26 calls, two measurements of one turn on two different scales. It reads **`26api·99steps`** everywhere now, and the shared tooltip says so in as many words. The real fault was underneath: the Console's live strip counted *rows since your last message*, which sweeps in every tool **result** (one per call, so the figure was roughly doubled) plus the permission asks and the "model changed" notices the panel writes for itself — while the tables and the Turns page count the transcript's own blocks. The same turn read in two places gave two numbers under one label, the strip's the larger. It counts what everything else counts.

**Every column tooltip in the Sessions tables got longer, and the sentence they all repeated is gone (S-17).** The right-click hint was appended to eleven tooltips on every row — one sentence, read once, printed several hundred times. It is said **once**, above the table (and by the selection bar, with the count, when rows are picked). What the cells gained: **Tokens** splits the total the way it is billed (in / out / cache read / cache write, and what share the cheap part is) and gives a per-API-call average; **Turns** separates the prompts you sent from the round trips that carried them; **Tools** says *what* it mostly did rather than only how much; **Cost** adds per-call and per-hour and names the model it was priced at; **Agent** carries the branch, the permission mode and the CLI version; **By** shows the entrypoint it was decided from instead of asking to be trusted; **Duration** says out loud that it is wall clock.

**A session's own page shows the shelf, and can change it (S-8).** A session was pinnable by right-clicking it almost anywhere it is listed, and on the one page that is about nothing but that session you could not even tell whether it was pinned. A ★ sits against the title, at the title's size, reading and writing the same marks store every other surface uses — and the labels you put on it are beside it.

**The capture box's position picker is never dead any more (S-12).** The queue holds **codes**, so a place can only be kept for an item that has one — but the panel was enforcing that ahead of time by greying the control out, explaining itself only to a pointer that hovered it, and silently throwing the choice away the moment the area went back to blank. The choice is always takeable and always kept; the warning is in words beside the box; and the position is sent to the file either way, so the host's honest answer — *"it went to the inbox without a code, so it could not be queued"* — is reachable at last instead of being written, tested and dead.

**And the saved-prompts filter has a ✕ (S-16).** Inside the box's right edge, where the text is. Esc still closes the whole window; this clears the filter and leaves the caret in it.

## 3.79.0 — Saved prompts has a section of its own, and it stopped depending on a default VS Code never handed back (2026-08-06)

**‼️ MEASURED, NOT GUESSED: the feature was starved, not broken.** `{"op":"prompts.list"}` on the running extension answered `file: null` — with the file sitting on disk at the very path the manifest declared as the setting's `default`. `getConfiguration().get('promptSnippetsFile', '')` returns empty when nothing is written into `settings.json`, so the reader had no file, the Console picker had nothing to offer, and the Farm Settings box drew blank. Writing the same path into `settings.json` by hand made the same op return both blocks immediately. The previous release blamed the schema's `format` keyword; that was the wrong culprit, and swapping it for `x-af-browse` changed nothing a user could see.

**The default path lives in the code now, not in the manifest.** `BUILT_IN_PROMPT_FILE` in `core/prompts.ts`, resolved by `resolvePromptFile(setting, exists)` — a pure function with its own tests. The setting is what you *chose*; the built-in path is what you get while you have chosen nothing **and it exists on disk**. The manifest default is the honest empty string, so on a machine without that vault the setting is empty because it *is* empty, not because something was deleted.

**‼️ Nothing is written into your settings on your behalf, ever.** The old reader put `""` into `settings.json` the first time the file was missing — and `""` is a value, so it outranks every default for good: one disconnected drive or one file renamed for a minute killed the feature permanently and silently. A chosen path that is not there is now **reported in words and left alone**: your setting still points where you put it, and the section says so next to the button that fixes it.

**Farm Settings → Saved prompts is a section, and it answers "does it work?".** It was one text box under *Display & Labels*, indistinguishable from a setting nobody had filled in. The section shows the file it is actually reading, whether that path is yours or the built-in one, **and every block it read out of it** — grouped by heading, with the whole text on hover and the line number it starts at. **📁 Choose a file…** opens your machine's own picker (markdown/text, starting where the setting points), **✎ Open the file** puts it in the editor, **⟳ Re-read** picks up an edit you just made in Obsidian, and **↺ Use the built-in path** *removes* the key rather than blanking it. The filter box finds the section by what it is — *saved prompts · snippet · template · kalıp · right-click · paste* — not only by its key.

**`{"op":"prompts.setFile","file":"…"}`.** Choosing the file is no longer mouse-only: the op writes it (refusing a path that is not there), and an empty `file` hands the feature back to the built-in path. `prompts.list` now also reports `source` — `setting`, `built-in` or `none` — so an agent can tell "you picked this" from "this is the fallback" from "there is nothing".

**And it reports `registered`.** Two releases went on the question "did VS Code accept this setting at all?", unanswerable from outside the extension host — one of them shipped a fix for the wrong culprit. `prompts.list` answers it in one call now: `false` means the manifest entry was refused, so the key cannot be edited in VS Code's own settings editor. Farm Settings is unaffected either way; it builds its rows from the manifest, not from the registry.

## 3.78.1 — The prompts file is picked with a file dialog, and the setting registers at all (2026-08-06)

**‼️ The setting was declared with `"format": "file-path"`, and VS Code refused the whole property over it.** `format` is a JSON-Schema keyword VS Code validates; given a value it does not recognise it drops the property, so `agentFarm.promptSnippetsFile` had **no registered default** — its box on the Farm Settings page drew empty, looking exactly like a setting nobody had filled in, and the Console's picker started with no file even though the default path was right there on disk. The marker is `"x-af-browse": "file"` now — an `x-` key is outside the vocabulary VS Code checks and rides through untouched. `test_settingsops.js` fails the build if any `agentFarm.*` setting reintroduces a `format`, or if one ships without a default.

**Choosing the file is the operating system's own picker, from both doors.** The Console's saved-prompts panel used to send you to VS Code's settings editor when no file was set — the extension talking about itself instead of doing the thing. It now has **📁 Choose a file… / 📁 Change file…**, which opens the native dialog (filtered to markdown/text, starting where the setting already points), saves what you pick and re-reads the list **in place** — the panel stays open and simply shows the new file's blocks. **✎ Edit the file** opens it in the editor when there is one. Same gesture as **Browse…** on the Farm Settings page, so the two doors behave alike.

## 3.78.0 — Five from the top of the queue: the two status-bar items, the tooltips that were saying nothing, where a capture lands, and the prompts you keep retyping (2026-08-06)

**‼️ The status bar counted AGENTS and called them "active" (U-4).** Two Console tabs on one agent are two turns, two costs and two things that can be waiting for you — and the indicator folded them into `1 active`. It counts **sessions** now, on both halves of the reading (the live monitor's `activeTurns()` and the index's new `activeSessions()`), and a session whose folder matches no registered agent is counted too: it burns the same quota. The tooltip says which number it is — `Active sessions: 3 across 2 agents`. Where the question really is about agents (the daily digest, the chat `/status` line, an agent row's ● dot) nothing changed, because those say "agents".

**The project item is a fixed width now, and the name moved into the tooltip (U-5).** `PRS.MuratVural_DEV_VSC.AgentFarm` is thirty characters of a bar shared with everything else VS Code wants to say; the one indicator whose text grew with its data was pushing the others off the edge. It reads **`◧ Project`**, always — and the hover carries the name, the status and kind, the session count, today's spend against the budget, the folder, any warnings, and four live links: **Open the project · Add to backlog · Change · Clear**. Clearing used to mean a quick-pick you had to get back out of. It also moved to the **right of the plan-usage percentages**, which are the figure read at a glance and keep the edge nearer the middle of the bar.

**Every column of the Sessions tables answers its own question on hover (S-6).** The row carried one tooltip — `right-click for session actions` — and a cell with no title of its own inherits it, so all eleven columns said the same eight words while the figures they were covering were reachable only by opening the session. Now **When** gives the whole date, when it started and how long it ran; **Duration** both endpoints and the minutes per turn; **Turns** what a turn actually is and what one cost in tokens, dollars and tool calls; **Tokens** the raw total beside the weighted one; **Err** what share of the tool calls failed; **Cost** the per-turn average and the reminder that it is API-equivalent, not an invoice. The right-click hint rides along as the tail — and follows the selection, so with rows picked every cell says what the menu would act on. Dashboard, agent page and the project's Sessions tab, from one component.

**A capture can say where it goes, in the same gesture as being written (S-12).** A small box left of **Add** on the Backlog tab offers the positions that exist — `1 · top` through `N+1 · last`, rebuilt on every paint. Touch nothing and the item goes wherever it would have gone anyway. It needs an area and says so rather than failing quietly: the queue holds codes and an uncoded capture has none, so the box is dead until the area beside it has a value. The line and the `## 🎯 Queue` block are written in **one guarded pass** — two writes could leave an item you asked to put first sitting last. `{"op":"projects.backlog.add","at":1}` does the same thing from an agent.

**‼️ The prompts you retype every day, one right-click from the box (S-11).** Right-click any button that SENDS in the Console — **Send · Send into turn · Send after turn · Start session** — and the phrases you keep come up in a searchable list; clicking one **pastes** it at the caret, still yours to edit. Nothing is sent by this menu; left-click still does exactly what it did. They live in a markdown file you own (`agentFarm.promptSnippetsFile`), edited in Obsidian with the rest of your notes: only text inside a ` ``` ` fence is offered, everything around it is commentary. A block's name comes from the fence's info string, the line above it, or its own first line — and the nearest heading groups them, never names them, so ten blocks under one heading are not ten rows called the same thing. The file is read when the menu opens, so a block added a minute ago is there. `{"op":"prompts.list"}` reads the same list.

**A path setting gets a Browse… button, and the schema is what says so.** Any `agentFarm.*` setting declaring `"format": "file-path"` or `"folder-path"` draws a picker beside its box on the Farm Settings page — no hand-written list of keys to forget to add to. The dialog opens where the setting already points.

**And the prompts file clears itself when it is not there.** The default points into the author's vault; on any other machine that path does not exist, and a default that cannot be right elsewhere must not sit there looking configured. It is checked once, on the first read, and cleared — the feature then waits empty until a file is picked. A path that is merely *unreadable* is reported instead: a locked file or a disconnected drive must not throw away a setting somebody chose.

## 3.77.0 — The Studio, the Airtable cards, and the audit that keeps all of it honest (2026-08-06)

**The Studio's scenes and assets.** A scene shows its assets, does the next thing it is waiting for (whatever that is — the row's button is labelled with it because it changes), sets its **status from a list** instead of a dialog, and copies its id. An asset opens its file, is **made again**, **varied**, given **feedback and made again**, **kept or dropped** — the verdict that lived on the Triage tab and nowhere else — assigned to a scene, and copied (path, what it was asked for, the model that made it). An asset registered from a watched folder **says it was not generated here**, so it is never offered the three things that would have nothing to act on. A provider row opens its setting or the vault the key actually lives in.

**An Airtable instance card** makes itself the default, reveals its folder and copies its name or path. An **analytics table** exports the rows you can see as Markdown, clears its search/filters/sort, and opens the task history. (The table, not its rows: those rows are aggregates and carry no id anything could act on.)

**‼️ And an audit that fails the build if the design rots.** `test/test_ctxmenu_reach.js` checks both directions at once: every section a page publishes has items in the catalogue, every section in the catalogue is published by some page, and **no page draws a menu without telling anyone it is there** — the `— right-click for more` tail is the only thing that makes a menu discoverable, and a row that grows one silently would be a feature nobody finds.

**The catalogue is readable by an agent.** `{"op":"ui.ctxmenu"}` lists every kind of row the extension draws a menu on; `{"op":"ui.ctxmenu","section":"afBacklogRow"}` answers "what can be done to this thing" — the same question a person asks by right-clicking. The words for a host command come from the manifest, so there is no second copy to disagree.

**The guide has a "Right-click everywhere" section** in both languages: the three rules, how to find the menus, the rows that are two objects at once, and the vault exception.

## 3.76.0 — An agent carries the same menu on all six pages that list one (2026-08-06)

**‼️ An agent is listed on six surfaces and every one of them had a different two or three buttons.** The Dashboard's cards, its comparison table, Portfolio, the Timeline's lane labels, the agent's own detail header, the memory graph's picker — right-click any of them now and you get **the same sixteen things**: open its detail, open it in a new window, start a session, start one after choosing model/effort/permissions, talk to it in the Console, continue its latest session, reveal its folder, open DUTIES.md, add a standing duty, add a memory file, open the memory graph, assign it to a project, distil a memory file, export an agent pack, sync it with Antigravity + Codex, and copy its name or its folder. **Every one of those is a host command** — a page publishes the row and gets the whole set without writing a line of handling, which is why the seventh surface will be free.

**A Timeline lane label is a session AND an agent.** Its name is literally what is written there, so one right-click now offers that session's actions (unchanged) *and* everything the agent can be asked to do. An agent row that only knows a NAME — which is all a lane knows — resolves correctly now, instead of falling through to a picker.

**The Dashboard's numbers stopped being posters.** Each stat tile says what it reads and, where there is one, which page answers the question it raises: **open the page behind this number**, or copy the figure. The plan-usage card gained **Check plan usage now · Reset token lap · Clear token lap · Copy the figures** — all of which existed only in the command palette.

**Also:** a memory file (on the agent page and as a node in the memory graph) opens, shows itself in the graph, and copies its path. A standing duty opens DUTIES.md and copies itself — watch and action together. A health finding opens the file it is about, opens the guide entry that explains it, and copies itself. A Living signal goes to what it is about, opens the check configuration, and copies its line.

**Deliberately not done, and why:** an insight row does **not** offer Fix / Delegate / Dismiss — those commands take the whole finding object, and a row carries a rendered line; a menu item that handed them a lookalike would act on the wrong finding and say nothing about it. And a Timeline lane gets no "show only this lane": the page has no lane filter, and a filter invented behind a menu item is a feature nobody can find.

## 3.75.0 — Reading surfaces: a turn, a step, a hunk, a held message (2026-08-06)

**Every session menu everywhere gained five items.** Copy the transcript path · copy the working folder · reveal the working folder · **open the agent behind it** · **add it to the work queue** (the box opens with the agent and the session already named). They are on the row wherever a session is listed — the tables, Turns, search hits, the Console, the Timeline, the Needs queue — and the two folder items are drawn only where there actually is a folder.

**‼️ A turn row and a search hit are each TWO objects at once.** A turn row was already a session row; now it is a *turn* as well: open the transcript at this turn, show its content inline, and copy what was asked, what came back, the whole turn, or where it is (session id + turn number) — and a copy asked for before the turn has been read **fetches it and says so** instead of putting an empty string on the clipboard. A search hit gets the same treatment: open at that moment, copy the matching line with or without its surroundings, and — the two questions a hit always raises — **search only this session** or **only this agent**, without hunting for the scope controls and retyping.

**The session viewer's four objects all became clickable.** A **turn** folds, unfolds, goes single-open, jumps, and — new here — sets its own **Export MD** state, including *export only this one*, which existed as a button in the breakdown table and nowhere else. The band and the table row publish the same thing, so it is one menu in two places. A **step** copies its text, its tool input or its result, narrows the view to its own kind, and **opens the file it touched** (recovered from the tool's own input — a step that touched no file is not offered it). A **changed file** opens, diffs against HEAD, reveals itself, unfolds its hunks and copies its path; a **hunk** copies what it became or what it replaced.

**The Console's two waiting states.** A held message: say it now, bring it back into the box, copy it, drop it. A live request: allow once, deny (or skip the question), and **copy what is being asked** — which neither row offered at all, and which is exactly what you want when the question needs looking something up.

## 3.74.0 — The Toolbox, the matrix, the settings and the vault (2026-08-06)

**The Toolbox is the page where you SEE what extends claude — and it could barely change any of it.** Its rows carried three buttons (open · folder · remove) and everything else lived on the sidebar tree, where the inventory is not in front of you. Now, from any row: open its file, reveal it, copy its name, its path or **how you invoke it** (`/name`, for the two kinds you actually type), and remove it. Per kind: **probe an MCP server**, show the tools it answered with, **enable or disable it for this agent**, **edit its definition**, **copy a skill or a server to another agent**, **move a skill between user and agent scope**, enable or disable a **plugin**, open its homepage. A plugin and a config file are correctly never offered "remove" — a plugin is switched, a config file is not deleted from here.

**The capability matrix's cells became clickable objects.** A cell is a claim — this agent has this skill / MCP server / memory file / duty, or it does not — and until now only the "does not" half had a control (the ＋). Right-click a filled cell to open the file behind it or copy its path; right-click an empty one to fill it. **The copy still names no donor:** the host compares every agent's version and asks which one spreads, exactly as the ＋ always did — that is the design, and the menu runs the identical message rather than a second one. A hole in a **user-scope** row is not offered a copy at all, because a shared entry has nothing to copy anywhere. Task cells and row names open the full task history.

**Farm Settings:** a setting resets to its default, copies its key, copies what it is currently set to, and **opens at the same key in VS Code's own settings editor** — where the raw JSON, the scope and the history live. An agent root reveals its folder, copies its path, adds another root or stops being scanned.

**‼️ The vault has a menu, and it will never have "copy the value".** Replace the value, edit the description, choose which agents may use it, delete it — plus copy its **name** and copy the **`{secret:NAME}`** reference a prompt uses, which is what you actually need when writing one. The value itself never reaches the page at all; there is a test that fails if a menu item ever appears that would copy it.

## 3.73.0 — The two boards and the blocker queue: triage without opening anything (2026-08-06)

**‼️ On Notices, acting on a message meant opening it — and opening one marks it read.** Every action a notice had (pin, star, hand to an agent, add to the queue, edit, export, dismiss) lived inside its expanded body, so going down the board deciding what matters silently cleared the unread state of everything you touched. From the right button none of it opens anything: **pin · unpin · mark read or unread · stars as a list (none · ★ · ★★ · ★★★) rather than a click-through · edit · answer a question · pass a question over · hand it to its agent · add it to the work queue · export it as Markdown · open the attached report · show what it said before it was edited · dismiss it** — and **Show only ▸** narrows the board to that agent, that kind of message or that level from the row you are already looking at.

**The work queue's safety rule is now in the menu, not just in which buttons happen to be drawn.** A live item can be edited, re-prioritised and dropped but never deleted; a handled one can be deleted but no longer changed — the row says which it is and the menu offers exactly one of the two sets. **Urgency is a list** (normal · 🔼 soon · ⏫ urgent) instead of the ⏫ button's three-press cycle, which needed three presses to get back to where it started. Plus: answer a blocked item, open the notice it came from, open the report it produced or the answer that was written, hand it to an agent, copy what it says. A **lane heading** shows or hides the dropped lane and copies the whole lane as Markdown.

**A backlog item can be quoted into the work queue.** Right-click a backlog line → **Put it in the work queue…** and the queue's box opens with the code and the words already written into it. (This was held back from 3.71.0 because the command took no text; it does now.)

**‼️ Needs You: a row that is stuck on a session now IS a session.** A permission wait or a stalled run carries not only its own blocker buttons but **the whole session menu** — interrupt it, end it, open its transcript, rename it, pin it, resume it in a terminal or in the Console, file it under a project. A row with no session behind it does not get those items at all, so nothing is ever offered that cannot happen. On top of that: open the agent that is stuck, clear a row you have judged, copy the row.

## 3.72.0 — Right-click reaches the Tasks page and Missions (2026-08-06)

**Everything you can do to an automation is now on the right button, including the things that were only ever in the sidebar tree.** A plan row — a schedule, a trigger, a workflow or a template — runs now, switches on or off (the menu offers whichever way the switch is actually thrown, never both), edits, renames, opens the task history, shows what it has run, and copies its name, its prompt, when it runs or its id. Two acts that had no way in from this page at all: **Export as a Windows Scheduled Task…**, and **Add a step…** on a workflow, which is what "edit" honestly does to one.

**A workflow's steps became objects.** They were a numbered list of grey text in the expanded row; each one now edits, moves up, moves down, copies and deletes — the four step commands that until now only existed on the sidebar tree. A schedule's "steps" line is prose and deliberately carries no menu: there is nothing there to move.

**A run row stopped hiding half of itself.** Open its output, open its transcript, reveal its terminal, start it, retry a parked one, run it again — **or run it again with an edited prompt**, resume the workflow from that step, save its prompt as a template, move it up, down or to the front of the queue, cancel it, delete it, and copy the prompt, the name, the id or the output path. **Show what started it** jumps to the Plan tab with that definition searched for, which is the question a failed run always raises and never answered.

**Missions: you can steer a run without opening it.** Approve, reject, answer its question, pause (or withdraw a pause that has not taken effect), resume, cancel — every one of those lived inside the expanded detail, so reading the list meant opening a run before you could act on it. Two more the page never had a button for at all: **run the mission again**, and **delete a finished run from the history**. Plus the lead's planner transcript, and copies of the goal, the verdict, the ledger or the run id. A **delegation** opens its prompt and live output, its archived output file, or the agent that ran it. A **round** folds, unfolds, goes single-open, and copies itself — the planner's thought and every step under it. A **participant chip** — the lead included, because the lead is one — opens that agent or starts talking to it in the Console.

**Copying reports itself everywhere now.** A copy changes nothing you can see, so a copy that said nothing was indistinguishable from a menu item that does not work. One clipboard call, in `media/afCtx.js`, used by every page: a page with its own flash line gets the message there, and a page without one gets a small line bottom-right that fades.

## 3.71.0 — Right-click starts meaning something, beginning with the Projects page (2026-08-06)

**‼️ Until now, right-clicking anything that was not a session gave you Cut, Copy and Paste.** Every row in this extension is an object — a backlog item, a task run, a notice, a skill, an asset — and everything you can do to one was either behind a small button on the row (narrow, and half of them only drawn in some states), or in the command palette among two hundred entries that say nothing about which row you are pointing at, or nowhere at all. Sessions had the full contract already: right-click one anywhere it is listed and you get the eleven things you can do to it. This release starts giving every other object the same deal, and the design and its running order are written down in `docs/SAG-TIK-MENULERI.tr.md` so it is a road, not a mood.

**A backlog item now carries its whole vocabulary on the right button.** State (open · started · done · cancelled), **Priority ▸**, **Size ▸**, **Type ▸** and **Move in the queue ▸** (front, ±1, ±5, back) are one gesture from anywhere on the row — including the rows whose badges are scrolled off, and including the distances that used to be a hover-menu on ▲. Plus: open it at its line in any of the four open modes, edit the words, file an uncoded capture under an area, jump to where it has been worked, and copy the code, the words, or both. Nothing was taken off the row; the menu is a second door to the same handlers, which is the rule the whole design is built on — **the menu sends the message the button sends, to the character**, and there is a test that fails if the two ever differ.

**The plan, the documents and the projects list got theirs too.** A plan item ticks and unticks from the menu (the same one-character edit, still no ✅ date written) and copies its words or its 🆔. A document opens in the editor, the preview, Obsidian or your own app, shows itself in the pane, and copies its path or its full path. A project opens, shows its sessions, jumps straight to its backlog, becomes the active one, reveals its folder and copies its name, its id or its path. A timeline line opens what it points at, or filters the feed down to — or away from — its own kind. A tracked run is a session, so it gets the session menu every other surface gives one.

**Under it, one catalogue and a test that will not let it rot.** A menu item lives in three places at once — a title, a menu entry, a handler — and when those drift the item does not warn you, it just stops appearing. `src/core/ctxmenu.ts` is now the single source of truth for all of them; `test/test_ctxmenu.js` fails if `package.json` has drifted from it and `--fix` rewrites it (splicing array elements in place, never reformatting the file, and refusing to write unless the result parses back to exactly what it intended). The pre-commit hook and `npm run package` both run it.

## 3.70.2 — Three controls on the backlog row that were lying about what they do (2026-08-06)

**‼️ The sort box was not broken — the page was throwing it away while you were using it.** Pick "alphabetical" and nothing sorted, every time. The ordering code was right all along: what happened is that with the **?** panel open (or any row menu), the click that opens the dropdown also reached the page's "click anywhere to dismiss" listener, and that listener **repaints the whole tab**. The repaint replaced the `<select>` you had just opened, so the native list shut itself and no option was ever chosen. The same repaint took the caret out of the search box and the cursor out of the capture textarea. A click that lands on a control now leaves the page standing; the control closes what was open when the gesture *finishes*, so nothing is torn out from under the pointer.

**‼️ The type badge answered its own question.** One press both opened the picker **and** wrote the next type in the ring — so the menu you were reading was already out of date about the item underneath it, and there was no way to look at the list and walk away. The badge had two click handlers, an old cycling one and the picker; in a browser both ran. The badge opens; the menu writes; clicking off it writes nothing. (The test that asserts exactly this had been passing for a release, because the DOM stub kept only one handler per event where a real element keeps both. The stub accumulates now.)

**▶ is a switch, and the row says which way it is thrown.** It sent `doing` whatever the row already was, so a second press asked for no change and got none — a button that worked once and looked broken for ever after. It is a toggle: **▶ again puts the item back to open.** And what it did was invisible: the only mark was a two-pixel border on the far side of the panel, declared *before* the queue's green border and therefore painted over on exactly the rows most likely to be started. A started row now carries **`▶ started`** among the badges that are always on screen, wears a tint, and keeps its ▶ visible without a hover — a state you can only turn off by finding the button again is a state nobody turns off. The hover says what it writes: `[/]` instead of `[ ]` on the line, which is what Obsidian's Tasks plugin draws as in-progress, plus a row in `Backloglog.md`. It stays live work; agents are still handed it.

**Packaging:** `npm run package` writes the VSIX to `dist/vsix/` instead of dropping it in the repo root.

## 3.70.1 — The backlog tab stops explaining itself in a wall and starts using its own controls (2026-08-06)

**‼️ The tab ended in a paragraph, and that paragraph was the biggest thing on the page.** Ten lines of grey prose under the last row, on screen for ever, in the one place nobody looks — a list that ends in an essay does not read as a list. The words earn their place (the queue is genuinely not obvious, and what gets written to your file is worth stating), so they are kept whole and moved behind **a ? the size of a full stop, next to the counts at the top**. Press it and the answer opens **in the page, under the bar** — no navigation, nothing to come back from. Click anywhere else, or Esc, and it is gone.

**Resting on `more` now shows you the whole item.** The hover used to be the native tooltip on the row: a grey box that repeated the two hundred characters already drawn on the line and then stopped wherever the platform felt like stopping. It is the item **whole** now, in a box as wide as the row, with a header saying which item it is and how long it is — and **the box scrolls**, and the pointer can travel into it, so a thousand-word item is readable without opening anything. For all but the enormous ones, hovering is now the same as opening, without disturbing the list you are reading.

**‼️ The filters are the extension's filters again.** Search, Dashboard, Queue, Notices, Tasks and the sessions table all filter the same way — a checkbox dropdown, **Select all / Clear** over a list of ticks, any subset you like — and this tab was the one exception: plain dropdowns that could say "all" or "exactly one" and nothing in between. Six axes are the shared control now (**state · filing · blocked · epics · plan · due**), which makes ordinary questions askable for the first time: *done AND cancelled*, *everything that is not a container*. **State is a real list**, so "open + doing" is no longer a fake state and **the separate "show closed" checkbox is gone** — tick done and it comes back. **"📥 unfiled only" is its own axis** (filing), because having no code is not a state. Sorting stays a single choice, because an ordering genuinely is one.

**The queue has a colour, and it is the same colour in both places it appears.** The position is the only thing on this page that is a rank rather than a fact about the item, so it is the one thing that gets marked: in **Next up** the first five wear a filled green disc, and on the row below the same number is green at the same strength. Past the op's default the same hue, half lit, across the rest of the strip; past the strip, nothing at all — those items are not waiting their turn, they are simply also in the file. One hue, two steps, and the type badges keep their own vocabulary.

**And "Next up" is a heading again.** It used to carry three lines of prose saying what the strip is, how to click it, how to drag it and which op reads it — every time, to somebody who had read it a hundred times. The line now says the two things that change (how many are queued, how many an agent takes); the standing explanation is in its hover, which is what a hover is for.

## 3.70.0 — The queue is one list now, and you can shuffle it like cards (2026-08-06)

**‼️ 3.69.0 answered the right diagnosis with half a design, and the half was the problem.** It kept the old order — the order of the LINES in your file — and bolted a "pinned head" on top of it: a short list that ordered the first few and let the file decide the rest. That is two orders pretending to be one, and it behaves exactly as badly as that sounds. Send something to the front and then push it back to eighth place, and the seven above it get dragged into the list as a side effect: **items you never touched acquire a 📌**. Meanwhile the file kept being reshuffled by ⤒ ▲ ▼ ⤓, so the sections stopped being readable as `K-1, K-2, K-3` and became a record of every "move this up" ever pressed. Two mechanisms, neither of them complete, and a page that had to explain which was which.

**There is one order now and it holds everything.** The `## 🎯 Queue` block at the top of `Backlog.md` is the whole queue: every open item, in whatever order you shuffled them into, areas interleaved freely. Moving one entry moves exactly one entry. There is no pin, no head, no tail, and nothing to explain.

**▲ and ▼ are buttons that are also menus, which is where the distances went.** Click either and the item moves one place — the cheap act keeps its single click. **Rest the pointer on it and a menu opens underneath: 2, 3, 4, 5, 10, 15, 20, and the very top or bottom of the whole list.** So "up eleven" stopped being eleven clicks without becoming a second button, which is what ⤒ and ⤓ were — and those could only ever reach the end of an *area*, which is not a thing the queue has. They are a row in the menu now.

**A row wears its place in the queue where the drag handle used to be.** The number IS the handle: it tells you what dragging it changes, and it costs no width.
