A **🙋 Needs You** button now sits immediately left of Export MD on the Notices page, wired through the existing shared `afnav` router — no new bridge plumbing needed, since navigation-only buttons are outside the bridge contract.

**The "N active • $X today" status-bar indicator now opens Live Timeline instead of the Dashboard** when nothing needs you — the running-now view its own numbers (active agents, live cost) actually feed into. The neighbouring plan-usage indicator (`3h 49m - 86% / wk 41%`) is unchanged and still opens the Dashboard at the top; the two indicators sharing one command was a deliberate design constraint, so this is a scoped, explicit divergence, not a drift — recorded as two separate command constants (`OPEN_DASHBOARD_AT_TOP`, `OPEN_LIVE_TIMELINE`) so the two contracts cannot blur back together silently. The warning state (bell, waiting count) still goes to Needs You either way.

- Tests updated for the new two-indicator contract. Suite: **85 files, 3814 checks green.**

## 3.50.0 — The composer stops lying about what it is sending, and ctx stops resetting (2026-08-01)

**‼️ The worst bug in the attachment feature was that it kept the files on screen after they had gone.** Attach, send, start typing the next message — and the chips were still sitting there. They were not attached to anything: `send()` had emptied the list, and **nothing ever redrew it**. The chip list was written by hand from three places and never from the render path, so it survived until the shell happened to be rebuilt. Meanwhile the next prompt went out **without** the files it appeared to be carrying. A surface that lies about what it is about to send is worse than one that never had the feature. The list is now a **slot**, drawn by `renderLive` — every keystroke, every data push, every send — and the suite proves it by reproducing the exact reported bug and failing on it.

**A drag repainted the whole page, every frame, for as long as it lasted.** `dragover` fires continuously while the hand is over the page and `dragleave` **bubbles up from every child the pointer crosses**, so turning the outline on in one and off in the other wrote the class dozens of times a second — a style recalculation and a full-page repaint per frame, plus a `getElementById` per frame, for a border that was already drawn. The highlight is now a **state**: it is written only when the answer actually changes, and enter/leave are counted so crossing a child is not a departure. `dropEffect` is set as well, so Windows shows a copy cursor instead of the "no entry" sign. *(This is a real, measured load being removed. It is not proof of what a display driver did during a drag on one machine — nothing here can prove that — but it is the one thing on this side that was hammering the compositor, and it is gone.)*

**Dropping text into the message box works again.** A page-wide drop target that claims *every* drag also claims the ordinary ones: dragging selected text into the composer had quietly stopped working. Files and URI lists are ours; plain text is only ours when it is let go **outside** the box. And a path dragged as text now has to be **absolute** — the old test was "contains a slash", which read every dragged sentence and every URL as a filename and answered each one with a warning about a file that never existed.

**The 📎 dialog showed folders and no files.** `canSelectFiles` and `canSelectFolders` together is not "both" on Windows: there is no such native dialog, so Electron falls back to the **folder** picker — every file vanished from a window whose only purpose was to pick a file, with no type filter row either. It picks files now, with the filters a file dialog is expected to have. A folder is attached by dropping it, and the tooltip says so rather than leaving a dead end at the dialog.

**Every remaining silent ending now speaks**, which is the complaint this feature keeps drawing: a drop whose bytes could not be read, a payload that arrived empty, and more files than the ceiling allows. The ceiling (`ATTACH_LIMITS.count`) is also applied **before** staging and against what is already on the message, so twenty-one dropped files no longer each get written into the temp folder before the twenty-first is refused.

- **A pasted screenshot keeps its suffix, and therefore stays a picture.** A paste often arrives with no name at all, and the suffix is what decides whether the model **sees** the image or is merely told a file exists. The clipboard's media type now travels with the bytes and supplies one. A very long name is shortened from the **middle** for the same reason — a plain `slice()` cut the `.png` off the end.
- **`consolehost.ts` was not a text file.** The filename-sanitising regex had been written with **literal control characters in the source** — a real NUL byte and a 0x1f — so git called the file binary and stopped producing diffs for it. Same rule, written as escapes.
- **‼️ The ctx figure collapsed mid-turn because it was being fed the wrong number.** `system:thinking_tokens` was read as a context estimate. It is not one, and the CLI says so itself: *"Live thinking-token estimate … `estimated_tokens` is the running total for the current thinking block"* (read out of the 2.1.220 binary). It restarts near zero on every block and is emitted constantly — **twelve** of them in one short measured turn — so a session truly holding 225k was repeatedly knocked down to ~100 and shown climbing 100 → 200 → 500 until the next assistant message restored it. It no longer touches the figure. Context is now measured with **`contextAfter()`**, the same function the sessions tables and the session viewer already use, and a zero means "not measured" so the last real figure stands.
- **‼️ And the session's token total was nearly double the truth.** The CLI emits **one line per content block** and repeats the whole message's usage on each of them — a `thinking` line and a `tool_use` line for one reply both carrying `in=10 out=3 cr=15642 cw=6961` (measured, haiku). Every line was counted. On a real transcript that is **55.1M against a true 27.99M — 1.97×** — while the sessions table showed the honest figure for the very same conversation. Each message is now counted once, keyed on the CLI's own `message.id` + `request_id`; one remembered key is enough because the repeats are always consecutive (measured over 403 assistant lines / 194 messages, zero exceptions).
- **A sub-agent no longer overwrites the context figure.** Its messages come down the same pipe marked with `parent_tool_use_id` and start a window of their own at a few hundred tokens. What it spends is spent and is counted; letting it write ctx made a 200k session read ~300 for the sub-agent's duration and snap back afterwards — the same false reading from a second source.
- **`agentFarm.consoleDropZone`** turns the page-wide drop listener off for anyone whose machine misbehaves during a drag. 📎 and paste never listen for a drag and are unaffected either way. It is categorised under Display & Labels rather than falling into "Other".

- 28 checks added, five of them verified by **putting each bug back** and confirming the suite fails: chips left on screen after a send, the highlight rewritten per frame, every drag claimed, "contains a slash" as a path test, and the chip list never reaching the render path. Suite: **85 files, 3813 checks green.**

## 3.49.1 — The 📎 that could be pressed and did nothing (2026-08-01)

**‼️ 3.49.0 shipped the attachment feature with its button wired to nothing.** 📎 drew, hovered and pressed like a button, and no dialog ever opened — no error, no message, nothing in the panel. That is the most expensive kind of broken: with no signal at all there is nothing to tell "the picker is broken" from "the panel ignored my files", so the only conclusion available to the user is that the whole feature is dead. It was, from the only entry point most people would try first.

**The cause was one line in the wrong function.** The click was attached in `buildShell`, which runs while `c-status` — the row 📎 is drawn into — is still **empty**; `getElementById` answered null and the listener bound to nothing. Even if it had existed, that row is rewritten on every data update, and a listener does not survive its element. **A control is now wired where it is drawn**, in the same block that already wires "End session" and "Open transcript". The suite gained the check that was missing on the way in: the button exists, it is *wired*, pressing it reaches the extension, and it still does after the row has been rewritten.

**Dropping was the same failure from the other side: silent.** Three fixes, one principle — a gesture that produces nothing must say so.

- **The drop target is the whole page, not the box.** A file let go two centimetres from a target nobody knew about is indistinguishable from a feature that does not work — and the composer is the smallest, bottom-most strip on the page, i.e. the least likely place for a hand carrying a file to let go. Anywhere on the Console now takes the drop; the box is still the thing that lights up, because that is where the file is going.
- **A drop that carried nothing is answered.** Empty drop, or plain words that name no file → a warning that says what to do instead. Same for paths that turn out not to be readable: previously `postAttached` returned quietly and the panel simply did not move.
- **A path dragged as plain text is now a file.** Some drags carry no URI list and no bytes — just the path as text. It is handed over, and the host decides whether it exists. **`Uri.parse` does not refuse `D:\zone\a.png`:** it reads it as a URI in the scheme `d` and hands back a path that never existed, so the attachment vanished with no error anywhere. The scheme test needs two characters before the colon, and a Windows drive letter has one (`looksLikeUri`, in core so it is testable without vscode).

- 20 checks added, driving the real `media/console.js`: 📎 wired and surviving a row rewrite, all four drop shapes (Explorer URIs, file-manager bytes, a plain-text path, nothing at all), the chip that proves a file arrived — its name, its size, how it will travel and its ✕ — and the drive-letter case that had been silently eating attachments. Suite: **85 files, 3768 checks green.**

## 3.49.0 — Files go with what you say, and the file decides how it travels (2026-08-01)

**‼️ The Console was meant to be the terminal's equal, and you could not hand it a screenshot.** Everything else had been carried across — steering a running turn, answering an `AskUserQuestion` in place, holding a message back — but the one thing a terminal does with a keystroke, pasting a picture of the thing you are asking about, had no equivalent here at all. The gap was never in the protocol: the console speaks to `claude -p --input-format stream-json`, and `userMessageLine` was already building `content` as an **array** in Anthropic message format. An image block always belonged next to the text block. Nothing was blocking it but the absence of a way to ask.

**The 📎 button sits left of "Open transcript", and the file type picks its own carrier.** Not a preference — a capability. An **image** (png · jpg · gif · webp) is embedded in the message as base64, because the model cannot open a file and has to be shown one. **Everything else** — code, logs, PDFs, spreadsheets, a whole folder — is handed over as a **path**, because the agent already has this disk and `Read` opens it. That is the better answer for a text file three ways over: a 10 MB log embedded is 10 MB of context on *every later turn*, `Read` pages through a PDF and walks notebook cells where a frozen blob cannot, and a file that changes mid-conversation is re-read instead of remembered wrong. One gesture for the user, two carriers underneath, and the chip on screen says which one is in use — an embedded file leaves this machine in full, and that should never be something you have to infer.

**Three ways in, because a webview cannot see a path.** A dropped `File` gives a name and bytes and never a location — browser security, not an oversight — so each route is handled as what it actually is: the **button** asks VS Code for real paths (`showOpenDialog`, the dependable route); a drag from the **VS Code Explorer** carries real URIs; a drag from the **OS file manager** has only bytes, which are staged into a temp file that then behaves like any other path; a **pasted screenshot** is already bytes and needs no path at all. Paste is only intercepted when the clipboard actually holds a file, so pasting text still does exactly what it always did. Staged drops are swept after a week — that folder is a staging area, not an archive, and nothing else would ever empty it.

- **A held message cannot carry a file, and says so instead of losing it.** The queue keeps strings (`shiftQueued`/`takeQueued`), so parking a message with an image attached would drop the image silently on the way. "Send after turn" is therefore disabled while files are attached, with a tooltip that says why. Widening the queue is the honest next step; pretending it already works was not an option.
- **Over the limit is a refusal, never a quiet resize.** 5 MB per image, 20 MB and 20 files per message. A silently downscaled screenshot still gets an answer — a confidently wrong one — so the send is refused by name, and **the message is given back whole**, text and chips together, because the composer clears itself the moment you press Send and a refusal must not cost you what you wrote.
- **The bytes are read at send time, not at attach time.** The composer only ever holds paths, so a 5 MB screenshot is not sitting in webview state — and a file deleted or swapped between attaching and sending is caught at the only moment where saying so is still useful.
- **Attaching to the first message works.** The button renders before a session exists, and `console.start` carries the files into the opening turn — starting a session *with* a screenshot is the normal case, not an edge one.
- **An agent can do it too, in the same commit** (`_REF/agentfarm-dev.md`): `console.send` gains `attach` (absolute paths), resolved through the *same* carrier rule as the button, so a `.png` behaves identically however it arrived. One agent can now hand a screenshot to another agent's session. A refusal comes back as an error, not as a success with a footnote.
- 31 checks added covering the carrier rule against real files on disk (image · text · folder · missing path · uppercase extension), the wire format (image block shape, images ordered before the question, a path written into the sentence in backticks, a screenshot-only message sending no empty text block), what counts as a message at all, the ceilings as one shared definition, and the bridge resolving paths the same way. Two existing console tests were updated where they pinned the old call signatures. Suite: **85 files, 3748 checks green.**

## 3.48.0 — A question you can always finish, and a reminder that does not outlive being dealt with (2026-08-01)

**‼️ The card that holds the turn had become the thing that made the turn unanswerable.** The Console answers an `AskUserQuestion` in place — the whole reason the terminal is no longer needed — but the card had **no ceiling and no scroller**. Four questions with described options are taller than a short panel, so everything past the fold was simply unreachable: the remaining questions, the free-text line, and **the Answer button itself**. The session then sat blocked on a question the user could see the top of and could not finish, with no way out but the terminal — a lock produced entirely by the surface, not by the agent.

**The card is now a column, and only the middle of it scrolls.** The header (who is asking, how much is still open) and the answer strip (free text · Answer · Skip) are **fixed**, so the way out is on screen whatever the questions do; the questions scroll between them, capped at 52% of the window. Three details, each one a way the naive version would still have failed:

- **The free-text line moved into the fixed strip.** "Neither — do it this way" is an answer a list of options cannot hold, and it is worthless if you have to scroll past four questions to reach it. Worst case — a card you never scroll at all — you can still answer in your own words.
- **Ticking an option keeps your place.** Every tick rewrites the card (the option lights up, the count moves), and the questions live *inside* that rewrite: answering question 3 of 4 threw the list back to question 1, which is the same bug wearing a different hat. The scroll position is carried across for the *same* request and deliberately reset for a new one — a question you have not read starts at the top.
- **The card says it is cut, and says what is left.** A scrollbar only answers "is there more?" if you already suspect there is, so the questions are closed by a line when they overflow — **measured**, not assumed, and re-measured when the panel is resized, since the same card is short on a tall window. The header carries **`1/4 answered`**, which is the one number a scrolling card cannot show you by simply existing.

**‼️ "developer is waiting for you" — and then you answered it, and it stayed there.** The idle-session reminder is a statement about a live *state*, so the instant you reply, close the session, or the agent picks the turn back up, the line on the board is false. It sat there anyway, and the once-guard made it worse than untidy: the key that stops a thing being announced twice also meant that **the next** time that session waited for you, nothing would be posted — the board had permanently used up its right to tell you.

**The farm now takes its own line back.** A host-posted notice remembers the condition it reports (`sourceKey`), and the reminder is withdrawn from the fleet's own state: still `waiting-input` = it stands; anything else — answered, closed, resumed, gone from the fleet entirely — = it goes. One test covers every way of dealing with a session, so the Console, a terminal, the ✕ on the card and the process dying need not be enumerated anywhere. The withdrawal rides `live.onDidUpdate` (hook events arrive while the transcript on disk is still being written), and it **frees the key**, so the same session waiting on you tomorrow is news again.

- **The user's own mark outranks the automatism.** A **pinned** or **starred** notice is one a person decided to keep, and nothing automatic removes it — but its key is freed regardless, so keeping a copy never costs you the next alert.
- Only reminders the *farm* posts about a live condition carry a source key; an agent's own `notices.post` has none and is never touched by this.
- 34 checks added: the card's three parts and what is in each, the count in both tenses, the scroll position surviving a tick and resetting for a new request, the overflow mark appearing and disappearing on measurement, a permission ask getting the same treatment, and the withdrawal — the verdict per key, the pinned/starred exemptions, other kinds of news left alone. Suite: **84 files, 3716 checks green.**

## 3.47.1 — The header counts stop lying: a number on screen never outlives what it counts (2026-07-31)

**‼️ Sixteen notices were deleted and the tab above them kept saying "🔔 Notices (16)".** The page's own subtitle said `0 unread` in the same breath — one screen, two numbers, one of them false. The cause was where the counts live: both badges (🔔 unread, 📥 needs you) are read out of `window.__AFUI__`, which the HTML shell writes **once, at build time**. Every mutation after that refreshes the page's *data* by `postMessage`, which does not rebuild the HTML — so the badge was frozen at the value the page had been *opened* with, on the Notices/Queue tab strip and in the 🔔 nav button of every other open page alike. It only ever corrected itself when the panel was closed and reopened.

**The counts are now pushed, not snapshotted.** Every page built by the shell registers itself, and the pair is broadcast to all of them from the same function that repaints the status-bar bell — so the bell and the pages cannot disagree, and no future mutation path has to remember a second place to call. The badge is a `data-afcount` element that is **always** in the DOM (empty at zero, or there would be nothing to repaint), so an arriving push is a text swap: scroll position, open menus and half-typed drafts survive it, which a re-render would not. `__AFUI__` is updated alongside, so the next render agrees with what you can already see.

- Every panel is `retainContextWhenHidden`, so a page in a background tab still receives the push; `postMessage` answering false therefore means the panel is gone, and its entry is dropped. A future non-retained panel would fall back to the old build-time snapshot — never to a broken page.
- A claimed nav button ("Console ▾" on an agent's page) keeps its count on the button, while its menu item stays the plain destination — `Open Notices`, not `Open Notices (16)`.
- 26 new checks in `test_header_counts.js`, driving the real `media/pageHeader.js`: the badge existing at zero, both places counting, a push emptying the 🔔 badge **without** re-rendering, the speaker config surviving a count push, and the host half of the contract (registration, broadcast shape, dead-page pruning, and the push sitting in the one function every notice/queue change already calls). Suite: **84 files, 3682 checks green.**

## 3.47.0 — Needs You holds only what needs you, and a row you have judged goes away (2026-07-31)

**‼️ "Daily budget exceeded: $180 of $30.00 (599%)." — what, exactly, was the user supposed to do about that?** The row's only action was a button that opened the Dashboard. It was a fact about something that had already happened, it could not be answered, and it could not be removed: the queue is *derived* from live state on every render, so it had no notion of a row being dealt with. It sat there until the day rolled over. Two failures at once — the wrong content, and no control over it — and together they are the one failure a blocking queue cannot afford: a page that teaches you to ignore it.

**One test now decides admission: "if I never touch it, does the farm stay stuck?"** Permission waits, browser approvals, missions with an open question, cut workflow runs, stalled sessions and Antigravity drift pass it — drift because no clock, no agent and no retry will ever clear it, only a human. Blown budgets, instruction suggestions, tasks parked for capacity and idle sessions do not: they are **news**, and news goes to **Notices**, the board built to be read and kept. Each is announced exactly once, and the guard is persisted — an in-memory `Set` reposts every still-open item on the next restart.

- **Clear a row: one ✕, a checkbox selection, or all at once**, with **↩ Restore (N)** to undo. Hiding rows silently would be its own kind of lie, so the page always says how many are hidden.
- **‼️ Clearing is self-healing; you cannot permanently blind yourself to a condition.** The key is the row's *identity* — session + kind of problem, with no timestamp and no message text, because those move while the problem stands still — and it lives only as long as the row keeps being produced. The condition ends → the row stops appearing → the key goes stale and is dropped. So the *same* wait stays cleared for its whole life, and the *next* one (a new prompt from that session, that session stalling again) arrives with a clean slate.
- **A browser approval cannot be cleared.** A real agent is parked on `await` behind it; hiding the row leaves it parked forever with no surface left to answer from. Its clear is **Reject**, which settles the promise honestly — the row carries 🔒 instead of a checkbox, in the UI and in the `needs.clear` op alike.
- **`agentFarm.idleSessionNoticeMinutes`** (default 30, 0 = off): an idle session never blocks the farm, but one left waiting all afternoon is a loop that quietly never closed, so it earns a single notice after a grace period long enough that ordinary back-and-forth never trips it.
- **Bridge:** `needs.list` (rows + keys + `blocking` + `hidden`), `needs.clear` (`keys` or `all:true`), `needs.restore`. 27 new checks — the admission test per kind, key stability across renders, the stale-prune round trip that proves a cleared row returns, the approval that a stored key still cannot hide, and all three ops through dispatch. Suite: **83 files, 3656 checks green.**

## 3.46.1 — The usage 429s, actually fixed: the edge was holding the button's privileges (2026-07-31)

**‼️ 3.45.0 claimed the rate limiting was fixed. It was not, and the reason was in the code that shipped alongside the fix.** The `User-Agent` diagnosis was right and the 60 s beat was applied — but the beat was never the only thing asking. `PlanUsageBar.fleetChanged()` fires on every live hook event, and on each idle/active crossing it called `service.get(true)`: **the button's path.** So an automatic reader, triggered by one of the busiest events in the system, held the *human's* privileges — it skipped the TTL, it skipped the very backoff that had just been added to rescue it, and the only thing left underneath was the 5-second hard floor. Configuring the beat politely was therefore irrelevant, and once limited, the edge kept hammering so the limit never cleared. Measured on the real workload (136 turns in the last hour; a burst of 12 quick turns 10 s apart): **12 requests, 10 s apart → 2 requests, never closer than 60 s.**

- **The three reasons a read happens are now named, because they may skip different things.** `beat` (the timer): skips nothing. `edge` (a turn just ended): skips the TTL only, floored at the user's active interval. `user` (⟳): skips the TTL and the backoff, floored at 5 s. Only `user` is a person; everything else is the extension deciding to spend a request on its own, and it does that on a leash.
- **Only the FALLING edge earns a read.** A turn *ending* is the moment the figures settle at what it cost — and the moment the beat switches to the slow idle window, so without it the settled figure could sit unread for the whole idle interval. A turn *starting* earns nothing: nothing has been spent yet, so the answer is necessarily the one the last read already gave. It was costing a request to learn nothing, and doubling the traffic on the busiest events to do it. (Both directions are still *recorded* — a rising edge that is not tracked turns the next fall into a no-op, and then the one edge that matters never fires.)
- **The edge changes WHEN a read happens, never HOW MANY.** Its floor is the user's own `planUsageActiveRefreshSeconds`, shared with the beat through one `lastFetchAt`: set 60 s and no two automatic reads are ever closer than 60 s, whoever asked for them.
- **An agent's `usage.plan force:true` no longer walks through a backoff either** — it takes the edge path. A looping agent cannot become a request storm, and the op's `describe` says so.
- 18 checks added, including the burst that reproduces the original bug (200 turn boundaries inside one backoff → **zero** requests) and the floor being the user's setting rather than a constant of ours. The old assertion that *asserted* `tick(true)` on the edge — i.e. locked the bug in — is replaced. Suite: **82 files, 3629 checks green.**

## 3.46.0 — The strip stops needing your permission to know what the turn is running on (2026-07-31)

**‼️ The strip only spoke when you had made a choice — which is the rarest case, not the normal one.** 3.45.0 put the turn's model and effort at the end of the live strip, and read them off the composer's own pickers. But a picker left on **"Default"** means *pass no flag*, so with nothing chosen the effort was an empty string and the strip printed nothing at all — while the turn it was pricing ran on a real model at a real effort, resolved out of `~/.claude/settings.json`. The model looked fine only by accident (`system:init` names it, so the picker gets overwritten); effort has no such event, so it stayed blank forever unless the user set it by hand. Asking the user to choose something in order to be *told* what is already true is the wrong way round.

**The CLI knows, and it will say so: `{subtype:'get_settings'}`** answers `applied:{model, effort}` — its own resolution of the whole precedence chain (command-line flag over local over project over user settings, capped to what the model actually supports). The host now asks at session start and again at the start of **every turn**, tagged with that turn's clock so a late answer cannot relabel the next one. It costs nothing: a local question about local settings, no tokens, no API call. Measured against the real CLI (2.1.220, 2026-07-31): no flags → `xhigh` off the user's settings; `--effort low` → `low`; **haiku → `null`**, i.e. this model has no effort levels — so an empty effort beside a known model is now an answer ("none"), not a gap. A CLI too old to know the subtype answers `error`, which changes nothing and is never shown.

- **The running turn is priced at its own model.** The live cost estimate used `model`, which a mid-session `/model` may already have moved to the *next* turn's; it now uses the turn's own.
- `console.sessions` gained `applied` (what is really in force) and says plainly that `model`/`effort` are only what was *asked for*.
- 13 checks added (the answer filling the turn, the null-effort model, a late answer about an older turn, an old CLI's refusal changing nothing and printing nothing, the next turn seeding from what the CLI last said, and the two moments the host asks). Live smoke §7 proves it end to end on a real process with **nothing chosen** and no prompt sent. Suite: **82 files, 3614 checks green.**

## 3.45.0 — The strip says what is thinking, and the usage endpoint stops 429ing (2026-07-30)

**‼️ The bug worth the version number: the 15-second usage beat was rate-limited into uselessness, and the interval was never the reason.** `/api/oauth/usage` is undocumented and publishes no limit, no `Retry-After` and no intended polling rate — the requests to document it were closed as *not planned* — but it has **two buckets**, and the `User-Agent` is what sorts you into one. A request with **none** — Node's `https` default, i.e. exactly what Agent Farm was sending — lands in an aggressively limited bucket and gets a **persistent 429 whatever the interval**, so polling less often would have fixed nothing. Three changes, in the order they matter:

- **The request now goes out dressed as the CLI's own.** Read out of the installed binary rather than guessed: `claude-cli/<version> (external, cli)` plus `x-app: cli`, over the same OAuth beta gate. It is the user's own token, their own account, and the very figures `/usage` prints for them in the CLI. **No process is spawned for the version** — every Claude Code transcript line carries the version that wrote it, so the newest session row already knows it; a machine with no transcripts falls back to a known-good string rather than sending a blank.
- **The active beat is 60 s with a 30 s floor enforced in code** (`agentFarm.planUsageActiveRefreshSeconds`, was 15 s / 5 s). A few-second setting is not a preference the endpoint will honour, and the community-reported safe interval with a correct agent is 180 s — this sits well inside it while staying far from the behaviour that earns the punitive bucket.
- **A 429 is answered by backing off, not by asking again:** 3 → 6 → 12 → 15 min cap, cleared entirely by one success. The **beat** obeys it; the **⟳ button does not**, because a person clicking it has decided to spend the request and a control that silently refuses for a quarter of an hour is one that has taught you it is broken. The state is said out loud in the tooltip and the card's note (`backing off, next automatic read in 6 min`) — a card deliberately going stale must not merely look frozen.

**‼️ The Console's live strip priced the turn without ever saying what was doing it.** It gave you every consequence of the choice — the seconds, the money, the lines — and never the choice, so "why is this turn slow / dear?" ("opus on xhigh") could only be answered by looking away at the pickers below, **which describe the next turn, not this one**. The line now ends with the turn's own **model and effort**, and it is turn-scoped for that exact reason: `/model` and `/effort` are both accepted mid-conversation and both apply *from the next turn*, so the strip snapshots them at turn start and a switch made meanwhile cannot relabel the turn you are watching. `system:init` is the one thing allowed to correct them mid-turn — that is the CLI naming the model we only had an alias for. The id is shortened the way every other surface shortens it (`claude-opus-5-20260514` → `opus 5`); an unrecognised id is printed as it came. Silence is meaningful: no model = the CLI has not said yet, no effort = the session never asked for one. **The finished turn keeps both**, so the strip does not lose them the instant they become final.

- **`⎋ Interrupt stops it` is gone from that line.** It was a permanent instruction about a button on screen two centimetres below it, already labelled `⎋ Interrupt` — the strip's most valuable column spent repeating the composer. The Esc key it also taught moved into that button's tooltip.
- **A fourth mini tool on the box: ⎘ paste — and it is never hidden**, because its work is in the *clipboard*, so the moment it is most wanted is the moment the box is empty (which is exactly when the other three correctly hide). **The one thing a paste button gets wrong is the space:** what you paste into a prompt is almost always a name lifted from elsewhere, landing right after the word you were still typing, and a plain splice welds them — `look atsrc/core/live.ts`. So the caret's neighbours decide: a word character immediately left and one space goes in first; whitespace, a newline, an opening bracket or the start of the box and it goes in untouched — adding a space where there already is one is the same bug from the other side. Only the **left** earns a space (the right is where the caret is left standing, so the next keystroke is still yours). A selection is replaced exactly as Ctrl+V would, and the caret lands after what was pasted.
- **A click on either status-bar indicator now lands at the TOP of the Dashboard.** The page is retained when hidden, so revealing it is not the same as opening it: it came back where you last left it — usually deep in the sessions table, several screens below the KPI row and the plan usage card the click was *about*. Both indicators share one contract (`OPEN_DASHBOARD_AT_TOP`); the palette, the nav and every notification still open the page without moving it.
- `console.sessions` gained `turnModel`/`turnEffort` and `lastTurn` gained `model`/`effort`. 53 checks added (the agent string and its fallbacks, the whole backoff ladder including the cap and the button's exemption, the beat floor, the turn-scoped snapshot through init/switch/hold-release, the strip in both tenses, and every paste case: welded, already spaced, empty box, newline, bracket, mid-text, selection, pre-spaced clipboard, empty and denied clipboard). The Console page test now stubs `parseModel` with the **real** one, so the strip's shortening cannot drift from every other surface's. Suite: **82 files, 3602 checks green.**

## 3.44.0 — The Console can be asked something, and effort stops being frozen text (2026-07-30)

**‼️ The bug worth the version number: the Console's own doc said "Claude Code does not ask the panel anything" — measured, and true, right up until it wasn't.** Every hosted session now runs with `--permission-prompt-tool stdio`, the flag that turns the streaming channel two-way. Verified against the real CLI (2.1.220) before writing a line: without the flag, a permission ask and an `AskUserQuestion` both resolve themselves as "nobody answered" and the turn just ends — which is exactly what the Console used to see, and mistook for "there is no dialog to answer". With it, a `can_use_tool` control request arrives, **the turn stays open**, and the answer sent back over the same channel lets it carry on — in the *same* turn, not a new one one had to be started to say the one word it wanted.

- **A card between the conversation and the composer**, the moment a turn parks on a question. The card, not `pendingPermission`, is the answer to a refusal now — and the row says `asking you` instead of the misleading `working` a turn that cannot proceed would otherwise show. Two shapes, and the CLI itself says which (`requires_user_interaction`): a **question** (`AskUserQuestion`) — its own options, one click each, multi-select ones collect until you press Answer, single-select ones send on the completing click, free text always offered for "neither — do this instead"; or a **permission** (any other tool) — Allow once / Deny with a reason. The measured contract for a question: the chosen label rides back **inside the tool's own input**, keyed by the question's exact text — `allow` with the input untouched is *not* an answer, it is exactly the "did not answer" the tool falls back to on its own.
- **‼️ Nothing is left holding a request nobody will ever answer.** Interrupting a turn or closing a session with a question still parked answers it with a deny first — the CLI is otherwise left waiting on an id that will never come, which looks exactly like a hung process.
- **Deliberately not on the bridge:** an agent can answer a *question* (`console.answer`) but can never grant a tool *permission* — the same boundary the general Needs-You queue already draws. Deciding whether a tool may touch the machine stays the user's call, in the panel.
- **‼️ Effort stops being a fixed label next to a live model picker.** It said, in words, "the panel cannot change this" — and it could: `/effort` is itself an `apply_flag_settings` control request, the same mechanism `set_model` already used, and it really moves (measured: `get_settings.applied.effort` went low → high → xhigh on the real CLI). A live effort picker now sits where the label sat, on the same pending/confirmed contract as the model switch. The one thing that is genuinely fixed once a session is running is the **agent** — the folder it opened in — and that is now the only plain-text item in the row.
- **The old permission box did not disappear — it got honest about which case it is.** Most refusals now arrive as the question card above; what is left is the one nobody was asked about at all (a permission RULE, a hook, an older CLI without the flag), and the box says so before offering the same live rule-change-and-retry it always could.
- `hostArgs` gained the flag unconditionally; `console.setEffort` and `console.answer` joined the bridge, `console.sessions` gained `model`/`effort`/`asking`. New `test_console_ask.js` (48 checks, pure) plus a rewritten `smoke_console_live.js` phase that spends real money to prove the whole loop against the installed binary: a Write asks, waits, and only writes after Allow; a deny leaves the file untouched; an AskUserQuestion parks the turn and the *same* turn — one turn total — carries on with the chosen answer; effort moves and is reported back. Suite: **82 files, 3550 checks green** (unit); smoke run clean, $0.18.

## 3.43.0 — The status bar tells the time it was pretending to tell, and follows the turn (2026-07-30)

**‼️ The bug worth the version number: the one number that looked like a time was the one thing the indicator could not tell you.** It read `5h 86% / wk 41%`. `5h` was a *label* — which window the percentage belongs to — i.e. a constant, i.e. no information; but it sits where a duration sits and reads as one, so the obvious question ("five hours of what? until when?") had a wrong answer built into the design. The field now carries the **time actually left in the session window**:

**`$(dashboard) 3h 49m - 86% / wk 41%`**

Both halves of the real question at once: how much is gone, and how long until it comes back. The week keeps its label — two bare spans side by side would be unreadable — and its reset stays in the tooltip, where there is room for every limit. A session window whose reset the endpoint did not state falls back to the word `session`, never to an invented span.

- **‼️ ONE BEAT, TWO WINDOWS.** These figures move only while a turn is **actually running**; a session that is open but waiting for your next prompt spends nothing. A single fixed interval is therefore wrong in both directions — too slow to follow the turn you are watching it for, too fast for a farm that is merely idling. The beat now follows the fleet, and one function decides it for every surface (`core/planusage.ts` → `refreshMs`, fed by the new `LiveMonitor.activeTurns()`): **idle** → `agentFarm.planUsageRefreshMinutes` (5 min); **active, at least one agent mid-turn** → the new `agentFarm.planUsageActiveRefreshSeconds` (**15 s**, floor 5 s). "Sessions are open" is not the question — `working` or running a tool is. `0` minutes still means off, and it means off in **both** windows, or the setting would be a lie.
- **The switch is instant in both directions.** A turn **starting** and a turn **ending** each earn one immediate read on the edge — the moment the numbers begin to move, and the moment they settle at what the turn cost. Only the edge acts (this runs on every live event), and the service's 5-second floor keeps a burst of agents starting at once from becoming a burst of requests.
- **The indicator owns the beat; the Dashboard card rides it.** Whatever the bar just read is pushed into an open card in the same step, and the card re-arms its own timer from the beat that came with it — so the card can no longer sit on a five-minute timer through the very turn it is meant to be reporting. Two surfaces, one figure, **one request per beat**, exactly as before. Both now say which window they are in — the card in its footer (`live · every 15 s while an agent works`), the indicator in its tooltip — because a refresh rate you cannot see is one you cannot trust, and "why is it not moving?" deserves an answer on the page. `usage.plan` returns the same `beat`, so an agent knows how stale a cached answer can be.
- **‼️ Three mini tools on the Console's message box: ⧉ copy · ✂ cut · ✕ clear.** They act on the **text** and never on the agent — nothing here sends, starts, stops or parks anything, which is exactly why they sit on the box and not in the control row below it, where every button talks to a session. Starting a message over, or lifting a half-written one out to paste elsewhere, was otherwise a keyboard-only job (select all, then a chord) on a page where every other action is a button. Bottom-right of the box, **left of the resize grip** which owns the very corner, half-lit until you hover, and shown **only while there is text** — copy, cut and clear on an empty box are three buttons lying about having work to do. **⧉ answers with a green ✓ for a moment:** cut and clear empty the box in front of you, so the box is its own receipt, but copy leaves the screen identical — and a control that answers a click with nothing has taught you it does not work. The caret returns to the box afterwards, so reaching for the mouse never costs the keyboard. (Two smaller honesty fixes fell out of it: sending a message and recalling a held one now repaint the composer row **at once** instead of waiting for the host's next data push.)
- 43 checks added (the countdown projection and its unknown-reset fallback, the beat function in both windows including "off means off", the edge-only refresh, the push that re-arms the card without stealing the ⟳'s spinner, and every one of the three tools: what reaches the clipboard, what stays in the box, that nothing is sent, the ✓ receipt and its lifetime). Suite: **81 files, 3497 checks green.**

## 3.42.0 — Your plan's two percentages, permanently in view (2026-07-30)

**‼️ The figure you need BEFORE you spend lived behind a page you had to open.** "How much of my session window and of my week have I burned?" is what decides whether to start the next long turn — and the only place that answered it was a card on the Dashboard, which means in practice you read it *after* the tokens were gone. The two percentages are now a **status-bar indicator**, in every window, while you work:

**`$(dashboard) 5h 42% / wk 67%`**

The status bar is the only surface in VS Code that is visible without opening something, which is what "always in view" has to mean — a second panel, a tree row or a webview all fail the requirement by definition. The two labels cost four characters and remove the one thing a bare `42% / 67%` cannot tell you: which is which. **Yellow** at `agentFarm.planUsageWarnPercent` (80), **red** at `agentFarm.planUsageDangerPercent` (95), driven by the **worst** limit on the plan — including ones the one-line text has no room to print. Hover for every limit with its reset countdown, the plan tier, when it was last checked and a **Check now** link; click for the Dashboard card; `agentFarm.planUsageStatusBar` turns it off.

- **Three honesty rules, because a permanently visible number is a permanent claim.** A machine with **no subscription login** (API key / Bedrock / Vertex) has no percentages and never will, so the indicator is **not shown at all** — one that says "unknown" forever is noise, not information. A login that **can** come back (expired token, a transient endpoint failure) stays visible as **`usage ?`**, because vanishing would read as "you have used nothing". And an unreadable snapshot is never coloured as if it were full.
- **One request budget, not two.** It shares the Dashboard card's `PlanUsageService` cache, so the indicator, an open dashboard and an agent's `usage.plan` together still make **one request per `planUsageRefreshMinutes`**; the card's ⟳ repaints the indicator in the same step, so the two surfaces can never show different numbers. What the indicator adds is keeping that beat alive when no page is open — and the setting now says out loud that one beat serves all three.
- **`Agent Farm: Check Plan Usage Now`** — the tooltip's *Check now*, the palette, and the Quick Menu's System group all call the one forced read; nothing is reimplemented. It reports the endpoint's own reason when there is nothing to show. (Already on the bridge as `usage.plan { force: true }`.)
- **The projection is pure and lives in `core/planusage.ts`** — no `vscode` import — so the text, the fallbacks and the colour peak are tested directly rather than through a mocked status bar. 24 checks added; suite: **81 files, 3454 checks green.** Verified against the live endpoint on a real account: `state: ok · plan: pro · five_hour 80% (in 4h 5m) · seven_day 40% (in 4d 5h)` → **`5h 80% / wk 40%`**, peak 80 (yellow).

## 3.41.0 — The same session menu everywhere, and a turn that does not vanish when it ends (2026-07-30)

**‼️ The bug worth the version number: what you could do to a session depended on which page you were looking at.** A session shows up in six places. The right-click menu existed in **two** of them — a Console card, a Timeline lane — and offered eleven actions. On a **Sessions table row** (Dashboard, Agent Detail), a **Turns row**, a **search hit** or a **mission's live feed** the same session offered *Cut / Copy / Paste*. So "how do I rename this?" had no stable answer; it had a page-dependent one, which is worse than none.

One contract now, on every surface (`media/afSessionCtx.js`, one `webviewSection == 'afSessionRow'` block in `package.json`): **Open Transcript · Resume in Terminal · Resume in Console · Focus Session Terminal | Rename · Pin/Unpin · Label · Copy Session ID | Interrupt · End · Clear from Live Timeline.** Two rules make it work rather than merely exist: **a row publishes only what it knows** — `sessionId` *or* the transcript path is enough, and one host-side resolver (`sessionFromArg`) fills the rest in from the index, which is what makes the *next* session surface free — and **the normal menu stays** (`preventDefaultContextMenuItems: false`; deliberately not a `contextmenu` listener, which would take the page's own menu away). Timeline cards gained the one item they lacked, **Resume in Terminal**, so both ways to pick a conversation back up now sit side by side wherever a session is listed.

- **‼️ The turn's figures outlive the turn.** The strip above the composer answered *"what is this turn costing me?"* while the turn ran and went **blank the instant the answer became final** — the one moment it was worth reading. The closing line scrolled away with the conversation, so *what did the turn that just ended cost, and how long did it take?* had no answer on the page. The finished turn now stays put until your next prompt starts a new one: **`✓ last turn — 41s · $0.031 (4.2k tokens) · 8 lines · your turn now`**. The figures are the **CLI's own**, so no `~`; it cannot be mistaken for the live strip (no motion, a settled glyph, and it says "last turn" out loud), and a turn that ended badly says which — **⎋ interrupted** · **⚠ failed**. The invariant is one sentence: `lastTurn` exists exactly while the ball is yours and a turn has already run — your next prompt clears it, a message steered *into* a running turn does not. On the bridge: `console.sessions` returns it.
- **‼️ Every command that starts a session asks WHERE it runs.** A session has had two homes since the Console arrived, and **Start session**, **Start session…** and **Continue latest** all went to a terminal without a word — so the newer half of the product was invisible from every button that starts a conversation. On the Agent page the three buttons keep their names and open a **two-item menu** under themselves (*In a terminal* / *In the Console*, the same menu shape the nav buttons use); everywhere else — palette, tree right-click, `Ctrl+Alt+S`, the Dashboard's and Portfolio's own buttons — a **QuickPick** asks the same two words and remembers the last answer. In the launcher it is the **Where** row, a dimension like the model (and in Console mode the Start row prints the launch summary, because a command line is not what runs there). Two things keep the Console path honest rather than approximate: a hosted session has no `-n`, so the name you typed is applied through Agent Farm's own title store **the moment the CLI reports the id** instead of being dropped; and `--continue` is a terminal affordance, so *Continue latest → Console* **resolves "latest" from the index** and says so when there is nothing to continue instead of opening an empty conversation that looks like it lost its history. `sessions.start` takes `surface` in the same commit.
- **‼️ Q is now free of Agent Farm entirely.** `Ctrl+Alt+Q` (Add to Queue) moved to **`Ctrl+Alt+A`**. On Windows Ctrl+Alt **is** AltGr, and on a Turkish-Q layout AltGr+Q types `@` — so the one binding the layout rule had exempted "for backwards compatibility" was precisely the one that fired every time an e-mail address was typed. The exemption is gone from the rule and from its test. (For the record: the extension has never bound a bare **Ctrl+Q** — that key is VS Code's own *Open View*, `workbench.action.quickOpenView`. A test now keeps it that way.)
- **‼️ Two rules in the keyboard test could not fail.** They were written as `condition || !console.log(detail)`, and `!undefined` is `true` — so the Turkish-Q AltGr check and the "the Quick Menu must not lie about its shortcuts" check both printed their diagnostics and passed anyway. Both now print *and* fail. (A rule that cannot fail is not a rule — and one of the two was guarding exactly the binding fixed above.)
- **The webview test sandboxes stopped hand-copying the shared script list.** Eight test files each concatenated `pageHeader.js + afFilters.js` by hand, so adding a fourth shared component broke five of them at once with an error that says nothing about the cause. `test/lib_shared_media.js` owns the list and **asserts it still matches `htmlShell`**.
- 73 checks added (the contract on every surface and the menu declared for it, the shared helper replacing three copies of the escaping, the finished-turn record in the reducer and on the page including interrupted/failed and what clears it, the surface choice on all four paths — dropdown, QuickPick, launcher row, bridge arg — the name-after-id and resolve-latest behaviours, the freed keymap). Suite: **81 files, 3429 checks green.**

## 3.40.0 — Controls that answer you, and a column that stays still (2026-07-30)

**‼️ The bug worth the version number: the money under the box stood still while the tokens beside it climbed.** `s.cost` is only ever what the **CLI has reported**, and the CLI reports a turn's cost when the turn **ends** — so for the whole length of a turn the one line under the composer moved on the left and read `$0.0000` on the right, and on a session that had not closed a turn yet it read zero outright. Two halves of one figure disagreeing is not a promise about to be kept; it is a broken number. The running turn's estimate already existed (it is what the strip above the box shows), so the session line adds it and marks the whole thing `~`: **`~$5.75 (480.0k tokens) this session`**. Nothing is double counted — the estimate is cleared in the same step that adds the CLI's own figure — and the base is the **session's**, so a resumed conversation's `~` grows on top of the earlier turns it came back with (3.38.0) instead of starting the sum over.

- **‼️ ⏸ Hold parks WHAT IS IN THE BOX, so an empty box says so.** Both composer buttons hand their text to a function that returns **without a word** when there is nothing to send. For Send that is invisible. For Hold it was the whole feature looking broken: the natural moment to reach for it is right *after* a message has gone into the running turn — and by then the box is empty, so the button did nothing, said nothing, the queue below it never appeared, and the held line with its **✎ ⏩ ⨯** was therefore never seen either. A control that answers a click with silence has taught you it does not work. Hold is now **disabled while there is nothing to park**, with a tooltip saying what would make it live; the first keystroke brings it and Send back. Send no longer eats the click either.
- **‼️ ⇅ What is allowed to move a card is now your call — and by default nothing is.** *"The list never reorders"* was written in the panel's own design note and enforced nowhere: the column arrived ordered by last activity, which with two agents streaming in parallel is a **race** — each writes one line, each jumps over the other, several times a second — so clicking the card you meant became luck. A small **⇅** beside **＋ New session** (the same language as the **⌕** at the other end of the column) opens a miniature panel with three answers: **`cards never move`** (the default: cards sit where they arrived and stay), **`waiting for you first`** (the only thing that earns the top is the ball being yours — *needs permission*, then *your turn* — as a lift, not a new home: answer it and the card drops back), and **`busiest on top`, damped** (a card climbs only after `after` separate signs of life, and the order is then **held still for `hold` seconds**, so the ping-pong happens once per hold rather than per line). Set `after` 1 · `hold` 0 and the old instant behaviour is back exactly — left expressible on purpose. Remembered; closed, the column shows nothing but the button, which carries a blue outline while the order is not the plain one.
- **⚖ The shared constitution has a button.** Mirroring the nest root's `CLAUDE.md` to the `AGENTS.md` files Codex and Antigravity read was reachable from the command palette and the `constitution.sync` bridge op only — so the one operation that touches **every agent at once** was the one you could not click. It now sits in the Dashboard's lower header row, among the destinations, because it is about the whole fleet rather than any single agent. It calls the same command the palette calls: the divergence checks, the modal for a hand-edited `AGENTS.md` and the `.afbak` backups are that command's, and there is one of it. (The header component gained one generic thing for this: a `related` entry may be an **action** as well as a destination.)
- **Every range button on the Dashboard carries its unit.** The first week was drawn as bare digits — `1 2 3 4 5 6 7` — beside a `14d 30d 90d` segment, so half of one control had a unit and half did not, and a lone "5" next to "14d" reads as anything but five days. Now `1d … 7d`.
- **‼️ A disabled button that looks pressable is the same lie as one that does nothing** — found by rendering the page and looking at it, not by reading the code. Nothing in `common.css` styled `:disabled`, so a disabled button kept its full colour and its hand cursor; the one page that had needed it (the resume header's ⌦) had hand-rolled its own rule, which is exactly the sign that the rule belonged in the shared stylesheet. One rule now, on every page — which is also what makes the Hold fix above visible rather than merely true.
- 43 checks added (the session line's live total and its `~`, the estimate being replaced rather than added to when the turn closes, a resumed session's estimate growing on top of what it carried, Hold and Send dead on an empty box and alive on the first keystroke, the whole held-message path end to end, the three ordering modes including the undamped equivalence and both damping parameters, the ⚖ button's markup and its route to the command). Suite: **80 files, 3356 checks green.** The Console and the Dashboard were also rendered in a real browser and looked at: `~$5.75 (480.0k tokens) this session` under the box against `~$0.250 (9.0k tokens) this turn` above it, ⏸ Hold and Send visibly dimmed on an empty box and live on the first keystroke, the ⇅ panel with its two numbers, the ⇅ button outlined blue while the order is not the default, and the range bar reading `1d … 7d | 14d … 365d | ⇤⇥` with **⚖ Sync constitution** in the row below it.

## 3.39.0 — What you type while it works goes INTO the turn (2026-07-29)

**‼️ The bug worth the version number: a queue that waited for the very thing it existed to interrupt.** Typing while a turn ran put the message in a queue and sent it "when this turn ends" — which makes queueing pointless by its own description: if it only arrives after the work is finished, you may as well wait and type it yourself. The Console said so out loud, and that is exactly what it did.

That was never a limit of the channel. **Measured against the real CLI** (haiku, a tool-calling turn): a user line written to stdin six seconds into a running turn was delivered to the model **at that turn's next step** — the CLI carries it as its own `queued_command` attachment, the same mechanism the interactive terminal uses for what you type while it works. So the Console does what the terminal does:

- **‼️ Send, mid-turn, steers the running turn.** "No, do it the other way" now arrives while there is still something to redirect. The line joins the conversation marked **↯**, because it did not start the turn above it, and the turn's own clock and totals are left alone — it joined a turn, it did not begin one. The button says which of the two it is (**Send into turn** while one runs).
- **⏸ Hold is the other choice, and it is a choice** — for "say this next, not now". A held message waits above the box and **stays yours**: **✎** puts it back in the composer to be rewritten (**↑** in an empty box does the same — the terminal's own key for its own queue), **⏩** stops holding it and says it into the running turn, **⨯** drops it. They go out in order when the turn ends. Before this the only verb was *clear*: a message you had written and could not change was, by then, out of your hands.
- **The bridge got the whole of it in the same commit** (`_REF/agentfarm-dev.md`): `console.send` takes `hold?`, and the new **`console.queue`** reads what is held and can `edit` · `drop` · `send` · `clear` it. An agent can now correct its own pending message instead of only wiping it.
- **🔒 The lock says which way it is without a tooltip.** 🔒 and 🔓 differ by a few pixels of shackle at 10px and both were drawn the same weight, so "is the row count pinned?" was answerable only by hovering. State is colour now, all on one side: locked is the accent at full saturation, unlocked has no colour at all (grayscale, faded).
- **⨯ in the search box.** A filter you set with one hand could only be unset by selecting the text and deleting it. The header's ⌦ clears the whole question; the box's own ⨯ clears just the words you retype, and shows up only while there is something to clear.
- 29 checks added (steering vs holding in the reducer, editing and taking a held message including the out-of-range clicks a repainted panel can send, the three per-line verbs and ↑ recall on the page, Hold vs Send from the composer, the ↯ mark in the conversation, the lock's two colour states, the ⨯ appearing and clearing). Suite: **80 files, 3313 checks green.** And the claim itself was proved where it matters — `smoke_console_live.js` now runs a tool-calling turn, steers it, and only counts the word if it lands **before** the line that closes the turn: **27/27, $0.112** with real claude processes.

## 3.38.0 — The model can change mid-conversation, and a resumed session remembers what it spent (2026-07-29)

**‼️ The bug worth the version number: "this session" was describing this window's process.** Resume a conversation into the Console and its totals started at zero — so the line under the composer said `$0.0000 · 0 turns this session` about a conversation with two hundred turns behind it, and on the first turn back it printed the running turn's tokens **twice**: once above the box as the TURN, once below it as the SESSION, the same number growing in two places. A hosted process is not the session, it is this window's turn at it. The totals now come back with the transcript — cost, tokens, turns and context, off the same index row the resume list already reads — through every door into a resume (the Console's own list, *Resume in Console*, and an agent's `console.start`). The strip above the box also says **`this turn`** out loud now: two numbers three centimetres apart, one labelled *this session* and the other labelled nothing, read as one figure disagreeing with itself.

- **‼️ The model is a live picker.** A running session used to be told its model "was decided when it started" — that was never a structural limit, only one nobody had looked up. `/model` switches mid-conversation in a terminal, and `set_model` is a control request the installed CLI really handles (verified against the binary: it is in the CLI's own set of handled subtypes, it validates that `model` is a string, and it answers success or error). So the composer offers it beside the permission mode, and `console.setModel` puts it on the bridge. The switch is **pending until the CLI agrees** — `set_model`'s success carries no payload, so the request id is the only link back to what was asked for, and showing the new name before the answer arrives would be a promise the panel is in no position to make. The session, its context and its transcript are untouched; it applies from the next turn. The agent's folder and the effort are still fixed, and are now stated as the plain status line they always were.
- **📌 Pinning showed nothing.** It worked — the trees were told, and only the trees: a star or a 📌 appeared on a page only when something else happened to force a repaint, which reads as *"sometimes it works"*. A mark is a visible fact everywhere it is shown, so every open surface (Console, viewer, dashboard, agent detail, portfolio, timeline) is now redrawn the moment one is set.
- **🔒 "show N" is not a filter, and stops behaving like one.** How many rows to list does not choose WHICH sessions, only how many of them you can see — so *clear filter* had no business resetting it. A lock beside the box pins it: **on by default**, the number you set is simply how long the list is, filter or no filter, and it is remembered. Click it off and it goes back to being part of the question, clearing with the rest.
- **A session can be found by its id.** `#407c78cb` is the handle every other surface shows — a card, a menu, a log line — and it was the one thing the resume filter did not search. Paste any part of one and land on that session. **Copy Session ID** is now on the right-click menu of every card, which is where you get it from.
- **Right-click reaches further.** Console cards (hosted and resumable) add *Label…*, *Copy Session ID* and *⌨ Terminal*; a **Live Timeline lane row and a live-sessions card** — which are sessions too — add *📌 Pin*, *Label…*, *Copy Session ID* and *Resume in Console* to the menu they already had.
- **The dashboard's range can be asked a real question.** The jumps were 7 → 14 → 30, so *"yesterday and today"* was unaskable and so was *"that week in July"*. The first week is now day by day (**1 2 3 4 5 6 7**), the longer spans are unchanged, and **⇤⇥** takes two dates — opening on the span you were already looking at, refusing a backwards range, and naming itself on the card (`Cost · 2026-07-10 → 2026-07-16`). Every path ends in the same list of day keys, so the KPI cards, the chart and the per-agent columns are exactly as accurate as the daily buckets they always summed; the weekly bucketing now follows the number of days rather than the old range number.
- 48 checks added (the model switch's pending contract and its accept / refuse / not-ours replies, the carried totals through all three resume doors and the turn that is once again a difference, the pinned row count surviving *clear filter* and clearing when unpinned, an id and a partial id finding a session, every new menu entry, the range bar's three shapes). Suite: **80 files, 3284 checks green.** The Console and the dashboard were also rendered in a real browser and looked at: `~$0.031 (4.2k tokens) this turn` above the box against `$0.123 (482.0k tokens) this session` below it, the lock lit beside `show 15`, and a custom range redrawing the chart across 07-10 → 07-16.

## 3.37.0 — ‼️ A conversation that was running, ours, and unreachable (2026-07-29)

**The bug worth the version number.** Reload the extension (or update it) and the Console session you were in becomes a trap: the Live Timeline draws it as running — *it is* — the resume list marks it as open elsewhere, and the shelf keeps offering **click to continue** on a conversation that can never be continued. Every part behaves correctly and the whole is a dead end.

What actually happens: a Console session is a `claude` the extension host spawned and reads over a pipe. `dispose()` ends it when the window goes, but an extension **reload** is not that clean shutdown — the new extension host starts with an empty session map while the old process is still alive, still holding its entry in the CLI's session registry. Claude Code cannot join a session another process holds (two processes on one transcript is the 3.29.0 rule), so the panel is right to refuse. Nobody is reading it, and nobody can. **Measured on the real thing, not deduced:** the registry showed the live `sdk-cli` pid for exactly the session this window had lost.

- **The way out, offered where you hit the wall.** Resuming such a session now says what it is — *"still running from an earlier window of Agent Farm (pid N) — nothing is reading it"* — and offers **End it and continue here**. The transcript stays on disk and `--resume` brings back everything that was said; only whatever it was doing that second is lost. It is asked every time, because it stops a real process, and the resume waits until the registry has really let the session go rather than racing a process that is still dying. Same offer from the Console's shelf, its resume list, and the side panel's *Resume in Console*.
- **‼️ Never a conversation somebody else is in.** "Our leftover" and "the session the OTHER VS Code window is talking in" look identical from here — both `sdk-cli`, both ours by id, neither hosted by this window. So each window now writes down what it is reading under its own extension-host pid (`console-hosting.json`), and a claim dies with the window that made it: a pid that no longer exists *is* the definition of a leftover. The offer is only ever made when nothing alive is reading it.
- **End session now ends the process the CLI registered**, not only the shell we spawned. The child Agent Farm holds is the `shell: true` wrapper, and a tree kill through it works only as long as the real `claude` is still a descendant; the registry names the process directly. This is what stops new leftovers from being made.
- **The filter's clear button moved to where the filter is** — a **⌦** button beside the **⌕**, lit when something is set and plainly dead when nothing is. It was a link at the bottom of a list you had to scroll to.
- **"Open" and "filtered" are two different facts, so they are two different lights.** One lit button had to mean both, so with the panel closed a narrowed list looked exactly like the whole list. Now: **⌕** lit = the panel is open · **⌦** lit + a **filtered** chip in the header = what you are looking at is narrowed. (Seen in the browser: the chip pushed both icon buttons onto a second line, because that header was never actually a flex row. Fixed, and the label gives way before the chip does.)
- **📌 Pin / Unpin is on every card's right-click menu** — hosted and resumable alike. The shelf is what the filter is *for*, and pinning is how a session gets onto it.
- 14 checks added (the leftover's four conditions and the one that must never fire, the wait for the registry, End session reaching the registered process, the clear button's two states, pinning on both card kinds). Suite: **80 files, 3236 checks green.**

## 3.36.0 — A card says what it spent in tokens, and the resume list can be asked a question (2026-07-29)

- **The cards lead with tokens, not dollars.** A 250px row has space for one figure, and the session's name needs the rest — so the money moved to the tooltip and the tokens took the badge: `482.0k tok`. Of the two it is the one that means something on its own (`$0.03` does not say whether that was haiku thinking briefly or opus barely starting), and the two strips under the conversation still carry the cost in full. The resumable rows changed with them, so both halves of the column read the same way.
- **‼️ The resume list was a fixed sentence:** your shelf, plus the fifteen newest transcripts. That answers "carry on with what I was just doing" and nothing else — last Tuesday's session was indexed, alive on disk, and **unreachable from the panel** unless fourteen newer ones had not happened.
- **Now it is a question.** A second, miniature button on the *Resume an existing session* row opens a filter inside the column: **text** (name · agent · labels) · **one agent** · **when** (24h / 7 / 30 / 90 days / *between…* two dates) · **📌 shelf** · **🧑 mine** (hides schedule / trigger / task runs) · and **show N**, how many rows you want. It is the Sessions tables' own vocabulary, deliberately not a second filtering language — shrunk to fit a 250px column.
- **The list says how much of the whole it is:** `20 of 1240`, with a **clear filter** link next to it, and the filter button stays **lit** while anything is set — even with the panel collapsed — so a list that is quietly hiding things always says so. An unset filter changes nothing: the default list is exactly what it always was, and **your shelf is never cut by the row count**.
- **The host answers the question, not the page.** The page can only filter what it was sent, and the whole point is to reach a session that is *not* in the newest fifteen — so the query goes to the extension, which can see every session (`core/consoleresume.ts`, pure and unit-tested). A **closed list costs nothing**: this snapshot is rebuilt on every event of every hosted session, so nothing is computed for a section nobody is looking at, and the list is capped at 200 rows however wide the question.
- **The left column is four independent sections now**, because the filter you are typing in must not be destroyed and rebuilt by an arriving line — the same bug the composer had before 3.31.0. The typed values are deliberately not in the markup; they are put back afterwards, and never into the box that has the focus.
- **Two things the browser check caught:** the header carried **Live Timeline twice** (the global nav has it, and the page added it again — the same duplication the Toolbox button had), and the line filter read **`show: show: everything`**. Both fixed. Ages in this column are now in **days** (`5d ago`), not `120h ago`, and a figure never wraps away from its label (`ctx` on one line and `132.0k` on the next is not a reading).
- 52 checks added (`test_console_resume.js` for the query itself — the shelf's exemption, an inclusive end date, the cap, an empty query returning the old list unchanged — plus the panel's contract on the page). Suite: **80 files, 3222 checks green.** The layout was also rendered in a real browser at 1200×760 and 820×460 and looked at: the column holds 250px, the page never scrolls, and typing in the filter survives a streamed line.

## 3.35.1 — ‼️ The wait was frozen at zero (2026-07-29)

*"The nudge is **time**, not animation"* is the whole design of the Console's left panel — and time was the one thing on it that did not move.

Every figure on that panel is written during a **data push**, and a push only arrives when the CLI says something. A session that is waiting for **you** says nothing, by definition. So `your turn · 0s` and `● 1 waiting for you · longest 0s` were written the moment the turn ended and then stood there — for two hours, if that is how long you were away — telling you the opposite of what they exist to say. They only ever corrected themselves if some *other* session happened to speak and drag a repaint along with it.

**The clock is now a reason to redraw, on its own:** the left panel re-renders every 10 seconds, so a wait grows while you watch it. Only the left panel ticks — it is the only time-dependent part of the page (the waits, the shelf's *ended 2h ago*, the resume list's ages) — and because the page writes into named slots only when the text actually differs, a tick where nothing changed writes nothing at all. 4 checks added (zero at the start, two hours later without any push, and the tick itself). Suite: **79 files, 3169 checks green.**

## 3.35.0 — The Console card carries its session's commands, and the numbers stop repeating themselves (2026-07-29)

Two things you do constantly in the Console had no answer on the surface you were looking at: **stopping the session you are pointing at**, and **what the turn now running is costing**.

- **Right-click a session card** — Rename · Resume in Terminal · Open transcript · **⎋ Interrupt turn · ⏻ End session · ✕ Remove from list**. Ending a session used to mean **selecting it first** (which changes the conversation you are reading) and then finding the button under the box; with eight cards in the panel that is three steps to stop the one under the pointer.
- **The menu greys items out instead of hiding them.** *Remove from list* is there from the start, in grey — it is what comes **after** ending, and an item that only appears once the session has ended teaches you nothing and changes the menu's shape under the pointer. *End session* greys once it has ended; *Interrupt turn* lights up only while a turn runs. The card hands its own state to VS Code (`data-vscode-context` → `enablement` in `package.json`, verified against how VS Code turns that object into context keys), and because `enablement` disables a **menu item** rather than a command, each handler still refuses politely when it is reached another way ("end it first, then remove it from the list").
- **‼️ Both strips said "ctx"** — the same session-wide figure twice, three centimetres apart — while the question you actually have about a running turn had no answer anywhere. Now: **above the box is the TURN** (`41s · ~$0.031 (4.2k tokens) · 6 lines · 2 waiting`), **below the box is the SESSION** (`session 407c78cb · ~24.1k ctx · $0.12 (48.2k tokens) this session · 3 turns`). Context belongs to the session line: it is what the window is holding, not what a turn did.
- **A turn is priced while it runs.** The CLI only reports what a turn cost when the turn **ends**, so the live figure is priced from the tokens seen so far at the rates every other cost in the extension uses, and carries a **`~`** until the real one lands — which is why a turn now keeps its usage **by kind** (a cache read is a tenth of the price of fresh input) and drops it the moment the CLI's own number arrives.
- **Cost is never written without the tokens that bought it**, in one order everywhere on the page: the money first, the tokens in parentheses. `$0.03` of haiku and `$0.03` of opus are not the same event. The turn's closing line now uses that same shape, so the estimate and the CLI's own figure can be read against each other without translating between two layouts.
- **Model and effort are stated once, where the launch decisions already live:** the fixed line under the composer reads `developer · opus-5 · high effort`.
- 37 checks added (the menu contract and its `enablement` clauses, the state the card hands over, the refusal when a live session is removed some other way, the two strips' contents, a turn keeping its usage by kind and starting the next one from zero). Suite: **79 files, 3165 checks green.**

## 3.34.0 — A swim-lane row is a session: right-click it, and Clear really clears (2026-07-29)

The Live Timeline had two surfaces for the same thing. The cards at the bottom carried every action — terminal, interrupt, end, clear, rename — and the rows at the top, which are the *same sessions*, carried none of them: a click opened the transcript, and that was it. So acting on what you were looking at meant scrolling down to hunt for its card.

- **Right-click a lane row for that session's commands** — Rename · Open session · Terminal · Interrupt · End session · Clear, the exact menu the cards already had (same `webviewSection`, so the two can never drift apart). The **whole row** answers, with the session it is currently drawing; on a packed row — sequential sessions sharing a track — **each session answers over its own span**, so the menu is about the run under the pointer, not whichever happened to be first. Left-click is untouched: the row name still opens the session, a bar or a dot still opens it *at that moment*. The hit areas are laid down behind them.
- **‼️ Clear left an orphan behind.** It took the card off the page and left the row — and because it closed the lane by writing an `end` event at "now", that row was usually **empty**: a session with no card, no events in view, and a single tick at the right edge. The one action whose whole purpose is "get this off my page" was the one that left the most visible debris.
- **Clear now takes the session off this page whole** — card and row together, in the detail ranges and in the 3d+ density view alike, and it **stays off across a window reload** (the ids live in `globalStorage/live-cleared.json`; the event store itself is never rewritten — it is the raw log). It still does not touch the session: the transcript stays on disk, no process is signalled, and a session that reports another event **comes back**, tracked from that moment.
